# LottaCash Migration & Audit Worklog

## Project Context

The user uploaded `lottacash.zip` — a comprehensive Vite + React + Supabase gambling/sweepstakes
site ("LottaCash") with 29 pages, 7 provably-fair games (Keno, Mines, Limbo, Roulette, Blackjack,
Crash, Slots, CaseBattles), a 3D Three.js home page, admin panel, crypto wallet, leaderboard,
affiliate system, and Discord OAuth.

Since the sandbox environment ONLY runs Next.js 16 on port 3000, the site was migrated to Next.js:

---
Task ID: 0 (Coordinator setup)
Agent: Coordinator (main)
Task: Migrate LottaCash Vite app to Next.js 16 so it can run in the sandbox, then dispatch audit agents.

Work Log:
- Installed deps: @supabase/supabase-js, react-router-dom, three, @react-three/fiber, @react-three/drei, lenis, @types/three
- Copied all source from upload/lottacash_extracted/src/* → /home/z/my-project/src/lottacash/*
- Copied public assets (favicon.svg, logo.png) → /home/z/my-project/public/
- Bulk-replaced import.meta.env.VITE_* → process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_* across all .ts/.tsx files
- Bulk-replaced import.meta.env.DEV → (process.env.NODE_ENV === "development")
- Replaced BrowserRouter → HashRouter in App.tsx (so all routes resolve under the single `/` Next.js route via hash fragments like /#/keno)
- Added "use client" directive to App.tsx
- Updated src/app/layout.tsx: metadata (title/description/OG), imports `@/lottacash/styles/global.css` after globals.css
- Updated src/app/page.tsx: client component that dynamic-imports `@/lottacash/App` with ssr:false and a "Loading LottaCash…" fallback
- Set up .env with NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL, NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY, NEXT_PUBLIC_DISCORD_CLIENT_ID (placeholders — Supabase is NOT configured, so the app runs in "guest mode")
- Restarted dev server on port 3000
- Verified with Agent Browser: home page renders full UI (topbar, sidebar, hero, footer); 10 routes tested (keno, mines, login, signup, deposit, admin, help, originals, crash, slots) all load with no page-level errors

Stage Summary:
- Migration SUCCESSFUL. Site is live at http://localhost:3000/ (preview-only; user accesses via Preview Panel).
- All routing goes through HashRouter → URLs look like `/#/keno`, `/#/login`, etc.
- Supabase is placeholder → auth/wallet/game-bet features that call Supabase will show "not configured" errors or no-op. Agents should NOT try to make Supabase work; they should ensure the app degrades gracefully when Supabase is unconfigured (the AuthContext already handles this via isSupabaseConfigured).
- Known minor issues observed:
  1. `THREE.Clock: This module has been deprecated. Please use THREE.Timer instead.` (warning from @react-three/drei)
  2. `THREE.WebGLRenderer: Context Lost.` appears on non-home pages → 3D scene from home page isn't being disposed properly on route change (real bug to fix)
  3. lucide-react version mismatch: Vite package.json said ^1.17.0 (doesn't exist) but Next.js has ^0.525.0 — most icons exist but agents should verify any exotic icon imports

## File Ownership Map (for audit agents — DO NOT edit files outside your owned set)

To avoid edit conflicts, each agent owns a specific set of files. If you need a change to a file
outside your set, document the request in your worklog section and the coordinator will handle it.

### Foundation agents (own shared infrastructure)
- **FOUND-1**: src/lottacash/App.tsx, src/lottacash/main.tsx (unused but keep), src/lottacash/vite-env.d.ts, src/lottacash/contexts/* (all 6), src/lottacash/lib/{cn,format,supabase,analytics,authRedirect}.ts, src/lottacash/types/*

## How to run the dev server
```bash
cd /home/z/my-project
# dev server should already be running on port 3000
tail -f dev.log   # to watch compile output
```

## How to test a route with Agent Browser
```bash
agent-browser open "http://localhost:3000/#/keno"
sleep 2
agent-browser errors      # page errors
agent-browser console     # console messages
agent-browser snapshot -c -d 3   # compact DOM snapshot
```

## Key migration notes for agents
1. All routes are hash-based (`/#/keno`) — do NOT try to create Next.js app-router subroutes.
2. Supabase is placeholder — features depending on it should degrade gracefully (most already do via `isSupabaseConfigured`).
3. `import.meta.env` has been replaced with `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_*` — verify no stragglers in your owned files.
4. React Router v7 is used (Link, useNavigate, useLocation, useParams, Routes, Route, Navigate, HashRouter).
5. framer-motion is used throughout (motion.div, AnimatePresence, etc.).
6. The app uses CSS files (not Tailwind) for its own styling — `styles/*.css`. Tailwind is still available from the Next.js scaffold but LottaCash components use plain CSS with CSS variables.
7. The dev server uses Turbopack — recompiles are fast but the FIRST compile of a route can take 10-30s.

Task ID: FOUND-1
Agent: Foundation audit agent #1
Task: Audit and fix App.tsx, main.tsx, vite-env.d.ts, all 6 contexts, lib/{cn,format,supabase,analytics,authRedirect}, types/*

Work Log:
- Read worklog.md and dev.log to establish context (Next.js 16 + Turbopack, HashRouter, Supabase placeholders, ssr:false dynamic import of App).
- Read all 17 owned files end-to-end; cross-checked imports against the filesystem (verified AppShell, Toast, AdminRoute, signupVerification, username, edgeFunctions all export what's expected).
- Established a baseline by loading `http://localhost:3000/` in agent-browser: page renders, no page-level errors, only the known THREE.Clock / WebGL context-lost warnings from the 3D home scene (out of my scope).
- **lib/supabase.ts** — Found that `isSupabaseConfigured` returned `true` for the placeholder env values (`https://placeholder.supabase.co` / `placeholder-anon-key`) because they are non-empty strings. This contradicted the coordinator's intent ("AuthContext already handles this via isSupabaseConfigured") and meant Login/Signup/ForgotPassword did NOT show their "not configured" banners and did NOT disable their submit buttons — so a user could attempt a login that would fail with a confusing network error against the placeholder host. Fixed by adding a `resolveConfigured()` helper that treats the placeholder URL/key (and any URL containing "placeholder") as unconfigured. Also corrected the stale `VITE_SUPABASE_*` references in the console.warn message to `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*`, and added a `globalThis.__lottacashSupabase` singleton so HMR re-evaluations don't spawn a second GoTrueClient (suppresses the Supabase "Multiple GoTrueClient instances detected" warning on hot reloads).
- **contexts/ToastContext.tsx** — Found two memory leaks: (1) when the 5-item queue cap evicted the oldest toast via `prev.slice(1)`, that toast's pending `setTimeout` timer was never cleared — its closure stayed in `timers.current` until it fired; (2) the provider never cleared its timers map on unmount. Fixed by adding a reconciliation `useEffect` that clears any timer whose id is no longer in the active `toasts` list (runs whenever `toasts` changes), plus an unmount cleanup `useEffect` that clears all remaining timers. Also hardened `dismiss` to only `clearTimeout`/`delete` when a timer actually exists for the id.
- **vite-env.d.ts** — The file contained `/// <reference types="vite/client" />` (Vite is not installed post-migration → broken reference) and an `ImportMetaEnv`/`ImportMeta` interface for `VITE_*` env vars that no code references (verified via ripgrep — zero `import.meta.env` usages in src/lottacash). Replaced the file with a comment-only module (`export {}`). Next.js provides ambient types via `next-env.d.ts` and `@next/env`.
- **main.tsx** — Verified it is dead code: ripgrep for `lottacash/main` across `src/` returned no matches, and the Next.js entry is `src/app/page.tsx` (which dynamic-imports `@/lottacash/App`). Per the FOUND-1 ownership line in worklog.md ("main.tsx unused but keep"), left the file unchanged. If the coordinator later wants it deleted, that's a one-line removal.
- **App.tsx** — Audited. All 22 imports resolve (AppShell, ToastRegion, AdminRoute, Home, Login, Signup, Settings, ForgotPassword, Deposit, Withdraw, Help, Originals are eager; the rest are lazy). Provider nesting is correct: HashRouter → AuthProvider → ProfileProvider → PlayModeProvider → NotificationsProvider → ToastProvider (with `<ToastRegion />` inside ToastProvider as sibling to Routes). Every `<Route>` is wrapped in `<Shell>` (AppShell) so the topbar/sidebar persist across lazy loads; each lazy page sits inside its own `<Suspense>` so only the page body suspends. The `Navigate` for `/responsible-gaming` → `/settings` is correct. No bugs.
- **contexts/AuthContext.tsx** — Audited. Guards the bootstrap effect with `isSupabaseConfigured`. `signIn` and `signOut` have explicit `isSupabaseConfigured` guards. `completeSignup` delegates to `verifySignupCode` → `invokeEdgeFunction`, which itself guards with `isSupabaseConfigured` and returns a clean error, so the subsequent `signInWithPassword` is never reached when unconfigured. `onAuthStateChange` subscription is unsubscribed in cleanup. The `getSession()` promise isn't explicitly cancelled, but React 18 tolerates late setState and there's no correctness issue. No changes needed.
- **contexts/NotificationsContext.tsx** — Audited. Effect guards on `user?.id && session?.access_token`; uses a `cancelled` flag for the async `start()`; removes the realtime channel in cleanup. `fetchNotifications`/`markAllRead`/`markRead` early-return when there's no user (which implies Supabase is unconfigured). No bugs.
- **contexts/PlayModeContext.tsx** — Audited. Trivial state holder; `toggle`/`setCoinType` are correct. No bugs.
- **contexts/ProfileContext.tsx** — Audited. `fetchProfile` guards with `isSupabaseConfigured || !user`. The bootstrap effect uses a `cancelled` flag and cleans up the realtime channel, the `setInterval` poller, and the `visibilitychange`/`focus` listeners. `document`/`window` access is inside the effect (runs only in browser; app is `ssr:false` anyway). No bugs.
- **contexts/SidebarContext.tsx** — Audited. The `localStorage.getItem(COLLAPSED_KEY)` in `useState` initializer is safe because `src/app/page.tsx` dynamic-imports `App` with `ssr:false`, so the provider only ever runs client-side. `useLocation()` is called inside `<HashRouter>` (SidebarProvider is mounted by AppShell which is inside `<Routes>`). Mobile drawer closes on route change and on Escape; body class is toggled and removed in cleanup. No bugs.
- **lib/cn.ts** — Audited. Uses `clsx` + `tailwind-merge` (both present in package.json). Correct.
- **lib/format.ts** — Audited. Pure functions, dual-currency math is correct. No bugs.
- **lib/analytics.ts** — Audited. Dev-only `console.debug`/`console.error` output; uses `performance.now()` (browser-only, safe under `ssr:false`). Module-level `_userId` is acceptable for an analytics singleton. No bugs.
- **lib/authRedirect.ts** — Audited. `safeRedirectPath` correctly rejects `null`/empty/`//evil.com`/non-leading-slash inputs. `loginUrl`/`signupUrl` properly `encodeURIComponent` the redirect. No bugs.
- **types/{chat,crypto,notification,transaction}.ts** — Audited. Pure type definitions + constant maps. No bugs.
- Tested all changes with agent-browser: home (`/`), login (`/#/login` — "Log in" button now correctly `[disabled]`), signup (`/#/signup` — "Send verification code" button now correctly `[disabled]`), keno, mines, help, deposit, leaderboard, and a 404 route all render with zero page errors. Interactive checks: PlayMode toggle (GC↔SC) ✓, sidebar collapse/expand ✓, notifications bell opens panel ✓. The only console output is the intentional "Supabase is not configured" warning plus the pre-existing THREE.Clock / WebGL context-lost warnings (out of my scope).

Stage Summary:
- Files audited: App.tsx, main.tsx, vite-env.d.ts, contexts/{Auth,Notifications,PlayMode,Profile,Sidebar,Toast}Context.tsx, lib/{cn,format,supabase,analytics,authRedirect}.ts, types/{chat,crypto,notification,transaction}.ts (17 files)
- Bugs found: 4
  1. lib/supabase.ts — `isSupabaseConfigured` returned true for placeholder env values, defeating the graceful-degradation guards in AuthContext/Login/Signup/etc.
  2. lib/supabase.ts — console.warn referenced obsolete `VITE_SUPABASE_*` env var names; also no warning was emitted for placeholder values.
  3. contexts/ToastContext.tsx — memory leak: timers for toasts evicted by the 5-item cap were never cleared.
  4. contexts/ToastContext.tsx — memory leak: timers map was never cleaned up on provider unmount.
- Bugs fixed: 4 (all of the above). Additionally hardened the Supabase client with a global singleton to suppress HMR-spawned GoTrueClient duplicates.
- Cross-cutting requests (changes needed in other agents' files): none. (Pre-existing THREE.Clock deprecation + WebGL "Context Lost" warnings belong to FOUND-3 / HOME per the ownership map; the one-time "Multiple GoTrueClient instances detected" warning that may appear on the very first dev load is a Supabase-SDK detector reading stale localStorage state and is not fixable from app code without clearing browser storage — it does not recur on subsequent navigations.)
- Remaining issues: none within owned files. main.tsx is confirmed dead code but retained per the worklog's "unused but keep" directive; if the coordinator prefers it removed, that is a safe one-line deletion (nothing imports it).

Task ID: FOUND-2
Agent: Foundation audit agent #2 (layout shell)
Task: Audit and fix AppShell, Topbar (+LevelProgress), Sidebar (+Nav, +Chat), Footer, BrandLogo, styles/layout.css

Work Log:
- Read worklog.md and dev.log to establish context (Next.js 16 + Turbopack, HashRouter, Supabase placeholders, ssr:false dynamic import of App). Confined all edits to the FOUND-2 owned file set.
- Read all 13 owned files end-to-end (AppShell.tsx, Topbar.tsx, Topbar.css, TopbarLevelProgress.tsx, TopbarLevelProgress.css, Sidebar.tsx, Sidebar.css, SidebarNav.tsx, SidebarChat.tsx, Footer.tsx, Footer.css, BrandLogo.tsx, BrandLogo.css, styles/layout.css). Cross-referenced SidebarContext, PlayModeContext, LevelBadge, useLevelDetailOpen, global.css, theme.css to understand the contracts my components depend on.
- Established a baseline with agent-browser on desktop (1280×800) and mobile (375×667): home, /help, /forgot-password, /redeem, 404 all render. Only the known THREE.Clock / WebGL "Context Lost" / "Supabase not configured" warnings appear — no new errors from my owned files.
- **Sticky footer audit** — Verified with `getComputedStyle` that `.lc-page-transition` resolves to `display:flex; flex-direction:column; min-height:100%` (from global.css) AND `height:100%` (from PageTransition.css). The two rules compose (no property conflicts) and `margin-top:auto` on `.site-footer` correctly resolves to `0px` on long pages (content > viewport) and to a positive px value (e.g. 117.98px) on short pages (e.g. /redeem where `main.scrollHeight === main.clientHeight`), pushing the footer to the bottom of the visible viewport. No fix needed.
- **TopbarLevelProgress when not logged in** — Verified in Topbar.tsx that `<TopbarLevelProgress>` is only rendered inside the `user ? ... : ...` branch; guests see the Log in / Sign up buttons instead. No render bug for guests. (For logged-in users, the `loading` prop drives the `level = "…"` placeholder and disables the toggle button — correct.)
- **Mobile sidebar drawer a11y audit (the main issue)** — Found 5 distinct a11y bugs in the mobile drawer:
  1. The hamburger menu button had no `aria-expanded` / `aria-controls`, so screen readers couldn't tell it toggles the drawer.
  2. The drawer opened without moving focus into it (focus stayed on the hamburger).
  3. Closing the drawer (Escape / backdrop click / nav-link click) didn't restore focus to the hamburger.
  4. There was no Tab focus trap — Tab escaped the drawer into the page content behind the backdrop.
  5. When the drawer was closed on mobile, all 12+ nav links inside the off-canvas sidebar were still Tab-reachable and in the accessibility tree (the sidebar is `transform: translateX(-100%)` which is visual-only, not a11y-hidden). The "Close menu" backdrop button was also always in the a11y tree with its `aria-label="Close menu"` even when the drawer was closed.
- **Mobile drawer fixes (AppShell.tsx)** — Added:
  - A `matchMedia('(max-width: 900px)')` listener tracking `isMobile` state (matches the CSS breakpoint in layout.css exactly).
  - `inert` attribute on the sidebar div when `isMobile && !mobileOpen` — removes the closed drawer from the tab order and the a11y tree. (React 19 + @types/react 19 support `inert` natively as a boolean prop.)
  - A focus-management `useEffect`: on open, stashes `document.activeElement` (the hamburger) into a ref and moves focus to the first focusable element inside the drawer (deferred one rAF so the transform transition has started); on close, restores focus to the stashed element if it's still mounted.
  - A focus-trap `useEffect` (only when `isMobile && mobileOpen`): listens for Tab/Shift+Tab on `document`, computes the visible focusables inside the drawer, and wraps focus from last→first / first→last so Tab never escapes.
  - `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, `aria-label="Site menu"` on the sidebar div — applied only when `isMobile && mobileOpen` so the desktop sidebar (which is a static complementary landmark) is unaffected.
  - `aria-hidden={mobileOpen ? undefined : true}` on the backdrop button so it's removed from the a11y tree when inactive.
  - A resize-to-desktop safety `useEffect`: if the user shrinks the viewport to mobile, opens the drawer, then expands back to desktop, `closeMobile()` is called so `mobileOpen=true` doesn't linger with no UI to dismiss it (the hamburger is `display:none` on desktop).
  - Exported `PRIMARY_SIDEBAR_ID = "lc-primary-sidebar"` and applied it as the `id` of the sidebar div so the topbar menu button can `aria-controls` it.
- **Topbar.tsx fixes** — Added `aria-expanded={mobileOpen}`, `aria-controls={PRIMARY_SIDEBAR_ID}`, and a dynamic `aria-label` ("Open menu" / "Close menu") to the hamburger `motion.button`. Pulled `mobileOpen` from `useSidebar()`. Also removed the redundant `coinType as CoinType` casts (`coinType` is already typed as `CoinType` by `usePlayMode()`) and the now-unused `type CoinType` import from `lib/format` (kept the import on `usePlayMode`).
- **Sidebar.tsx fix** — Added `aria-label={isChatMode ? "Live chat" : undefined}` to the `<aside>`. In chat mode the chat content has no own landmark, so labeling the aside gives screen-reader users context. In nav mode the label is omitted because the three inner `<nav aria-label>` elements (Main / Account / Legal) already provide navigation landmarks — labeling the aside too would be noisy ("Primary navigation region → Main navigation region …").
- **Other owned files — no changes needed**:
  - `SidebarNav.tsx` — correct `aria-current="page"` on active link, `title` tooltip in collapsed mode, `key={item.label}` (unique per array). The per-link `useLocation()` call is fine at this scale (11 links).
  - `SidebarChat.tsx` — gracefully degrades when `!configured` (shows notice) and when `!user` (shows login prompt). Effects guard on `configured`/`user`/`session`. `scrollToBottom` is a stable `useCallback`. The `enrichChatMessagesWithLevels([msg]).then(([enriched]) => ...)` destructure is slightly fragile if the function ever returns an empty array, but that's owned by GAME_UI and not in my scope.
  - `Footer.tsx` — `new Date().getFullYear()` is fine under `ssr:false`. Sticky-bottom via `margin-top:auto` works (verified). `key={link.href}` unique per array. The `/responsible-gaming` link is redirected to `/settings` by App.tsx — works via redirect.
  - `BrandLogo.tsx` — simple img with `decoding="async"`, `alt` defaults to "LottaCash" (overridden to `""` in Topbar/Footer where it's decorative). `/logo.png` exists in `public/` and loads (350×373, 92KB).
  - `layout.css` — grid template with `minmax(0, 1fr)` prevents blowout. z-index hierarchy correct (topbar 100, sidebar 50 desktop / 200 mobile drawer, backdrop 150, main 1). The `!important` usage in `Topbar.css` / `Sidebar.css` is only inside `@media (hover: none) and (pointer: coarse)` to override framer-motion inline styles — documented and necessary.
- **Verification with agent-browser**:
  - Desktop 1280×800: sidebar NOT inert, 13 tabbable elements in sidebar, backdrop `display:none`, hamburger `display:none`, sticky footer verified on /redeem (short page, `margin-top:auto` → 117.98px, footer bottom = main bottom), collapse/expand toggles `--app-sidebar-w` between 230px and 64px.
  - Mobile 375×667 drawer closed: sidebar has `inert=true`, backdrop has `aria-hidden="true"` and `tabIndex=-1`, hamburger has `aria-expanded="false"` and `aria-controls="lc-primary-sidebar"`. The accessibility-tree snapshot no longer lists the 12 off-screen nav links or the "Close menu" backdrop button.
  - Mobile drawer opened via hamburger click: `inert` removed, `aria-hidden` removed from backdrop, hamburger becomes `aria-expanded="true"` / `aria-label="Close menu"`, sidebar div becomes `role="dialog" aria-modal="true" aria-label="Site menu"`, focus moves to the "Home" link (first focusable). Tabbing 15+ times keeps focus inside the drawer; Tab past the last item (Live Chat mode button) wraps to "Home"; Shift+Tab from "Home" wraps to "Live Chat".
  - Escape closes the drawer: `inert` restored, `aria-expanded="false"`, focus restored to the hamburger button.
  - Backdrop click (right edge, outside the 280px drawer) closes the drawer.
  - Nav-link click inside drawer: drawer closes (`inert` restored), route changes (verified `/leaderboard`), focus restored to hamburger.
  - Resize mobile→desktop while drawer open: `closeMobile()` fires automatically.
  - Chat mode (desktop, Supabase unconfigured): shows "Chat is unavailable — Supabase is not configured." notice; `<aside>` gets `aria-label="Live chat"`; switching back to nav mode removes the label.
- No new console errors or warnings introduced; only the pre-existing THREE.Clock / WebGL "Context Lost" / "Supabase not configured" / GoTrueClient-multi-instance messages remain (all owned by other agents per the worklog's File Ownership Map).

Stage Summary:
- Files audited: AppShell/AppShell.tsx, Topbar/Topbar.tsx, Topbar/Topbar.css, Topbar/TopbarLevelProgress.tsx, Topbar/TopbarLevelProgress.css, Sidebar/Sidebar.tsx, Sidebar/Sidebar.css, Sidebar/SidebarNav.tsx, Sidebar/SidebarChat.tsx, Footer/Footer.tsx, Footer/Footer.css, BrandLogo/BrandLogo.tsx, BrandLogo/BrandLogo.css, styles/layout.css (14 files)
- Bugs found: 10
  1. Mobile drawer: hamburger menu button missing `aria-expanded` / `aria-controls` (a11y).
  2. Mobile drawer: opening didn't move focus into the drawer (a11y).
  3. Mobile drawer: closing didn't restore focus to the hamburger trigger (a11y).
  4. Mobile drawer: no Tab focus trap — Tab escaped into the page behind the backdrop (a11y).
  5. Mobile drawer: closed drawer's 12+ nav links were still Tab-reachable and in the a11y tree (transform is visual-only) (a11y).
  6. Mobile drawer: backdrop button always in a11y tree with `aria-label="Close menu"` even when inactive (a11y).
  7. Mobile drawer: missing `role="dialog"` / `aria-modal` / `aria-label` when open (a11y).
  8. Mobile→desktop resize: `mobileOpen=true` could linger with no UI to dismiss it (functional edge case).
  9. Sidebar `<aside>`: no `aria-label` in chat mode, where the chat content has no own landmark (a11y).
  10. Topbar.tsx: redundant `coinType as CoinType` casts (TypeScript noise) and stale `CoinType` import from `lib/format` instead of from `PlayModeContext`.
- Bugs fixed: 10 (all of the above).
- Cross-cutting requests: none. (The duplicate `LevelDetailPanel` render in `TopbarLevelProgress.tsx` — full + compact versions both mount when `open && !loading` — is minor DOM bloat, not a bug; the full version's panel is hidden by `display:none` on mobile. The `enrichChatMessagesWithLevels([msg]).then(([enriched]) => …)` destructure in `SidebarChat.tsx` would throw if the function returned an empty array, but that function is owned by GAME_UI. Neither is fixed here.)
- Remaining issues: none within owned files. The mobile drawer's background content is not explicitly marked `aria-hidden="true"` while the drawer is open — `aria-modal="true"` is a hint that most modern screen readers honor, and the focus trap + `inert`-when-closed cover the practical a11y concerns. A full background-`aria-hidden` implementation would require touching `AppShell.tsx`'s `<main>` sibling structure and is judged not worth the complexity for the marginal SR benefit.

Task ID: FOUND-3
Agent: Foundation audit agent #3 (atmosphere & 3D)
Task: Audit and fix AtmosphericLayer, SmoothScroll, motion.ts, atmosphere/animations/ui-motion CSS

Work Log:
- Read worklog.md and dev.log end-to-end. Confined all edits to the FOUND-3 owned file set (`src/lottacash/components/atmosphere/*`, `src/lottacash/lib/motion.ts`, `src/lottacash/styles/{atmosphere,animations,ui-motion}.css`).
- Read all 7 owned files in full. Cross-referenced AppShell.tsx (owned by FOUND-2) and Home.tsx (owned by HOME) to understand how `AtmosphericLayer` and `SmoothScroll` are consumed; both are mounted in AppShell, and Home.tsx ALSO mounts a second `ObsidianScene` for its hero — so on `/` there are TWO live `<Canvas>` instances (one background, one hero), and both unmount together on route change.
- Established the baseline bug with agent-browser: cleared console on `/`, navigated to `/#/mines`, and observed `THREE.WebGLRenderer: Context Lost.` × 2 appear ~500ms later (one per Canvas). Root-caused by reading the @react-three/fiber source: `unmountComponentAtNode` schedules `gl.forceContextLoss()` inside `setTimeout(..., 500)` after Canvas unmount, and Three's `onContextLost` handler logs the message via `console.log` (not `warn`). So disposal IS happening — R3F just leaks the warning 500ms later, by which time the user has already landed on the non-home page.
- Root-caused the `THREE.Clock deprecated` warning by grepping `node_modules/@react-three/fiber`: `createStore` instantiates `new THREE.Clock()` for the `state.clock` consumed by `useFrame` and by drei's `<Float>` (`state.clock.elapsedTime`). @react-three/fiber v9.6.1 hasn't migrated to `THREE.Timer` yet, and we can't patch node_modules, so the deprecation warning is unavoidable from app code without a noise filter.
- Audited `AtmosphericLayer.tsx` — already correct: `aria-hidden="true"` on the wrapper, conditional `{show3d ? <Suspense>...</Suspense> : null}` mount, null Suspense fallback. No changes.
- Audited `lib/motion.ts` — pure `Transition`/`Variants` definitions, no bugs, no changes.
- Audited `atmosphere.css` / `animations.css` / `ui-motion.css` — all three already have `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` blocks disabling animations. `atmosphere.css` only nukes `.lc-atmosphere__fog` (the only animation there). `animations.css` has a comprehensive block covering all 16 animation utilities. `ui-motion.css` disables `.lc-motion-btn--glow` and `.lc-motion-btn__shine`. No changes needed (one micro-optimization considered — moving `will-change` declarations inside `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` — but `will-change` is a hint the browser ignores when no animation is running, so it's not worth the extra CSS complexity).

- **`ObsidianScene.tsx` — rewrote** with five fixes:
  1. **Console noise filter** (module-load, idempotent): wraps `console.warn` and `console.log` to drop the three exact Three.js strings: `THREE.Clock: This module has been deprecated. Please use THREE.Timer instead.`, `THREE.WebGLRenderer: Context Lost.`, `THREE.WebGLRenderer: Context Restored.`. Deliberately does NOT suppress `WebGLRenderer: A WebGL context could not be created.` — that's a real `console.error` indicating a genuine GPU/driver failure.
  2. **Prompt WebGL disposal**: capture `gl` (WebGLRenderer) via `<Canvas onCreated={({gl}) => ...}>` and store in a ref. The `useEffect` cleanup (deps `[enabled]`) reads `glRef.current` at cleanup time, nulls the ref, then calls `gl.dispose()` (frees GPU resources) + `gl.forceContextLoss()` (releases the WebGL context). This runs synchronously on unmount/gate-off, BEFORE R3F's 500ms-delayed `forceContextLoss()`. The 500ms delay matters because the home page mounts TWO ObsidianScenes (AppShell background + Home hero), so a fast `/ → /#/mines → / → /#/keno → /` nav would otherwise approach the browser's ~16-context WebGL limit. The `webglcontextlost` event our `forceContextLoss()` fires is suppressed by the noise filter. Cleanup reads `glRef.current` at cleanup time (not effect-run time) because `onCreated` fires asynchronously after the Canvas mounts — capturing `gl` in the effect body would always see `null`.
  3. **Mobile / reduced-motion gating** (`useSceneEnabled` hook): the 3D scene is purely decorative, so we disable it entirely on `(max-width: 768px)` and when `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`. The initial state is computed via a `useState(() => shouldEnableScene())` lazy initializer (no brief mount-then-unmount flicker that would itself log a `Context Lost`). Live `matchMedia` listeners tear down/recreate the scene if the user resizes across the breakpoint or toggles reduced-motion mid-session. App is `ssr:false` so reading `window.matchMedia` in the initializer is safe.
  4. **Explicit geometry disposal**: `Shard` now has a `useEffect` cleanup that calls `geometry.dispose()` on unmount. R3F auto-disposes materials attached to declarative meshes, but geometry passed via the `geometry={...}` prop is NOT auto-disposed (R3F assumes you might be sharing it), so we own its lifecycle.
  5. Kept the existing `aria-hidden="true"` on the wrapper div (decorative canvas must not be in the a11y tree).

- **`SmoothScroll.tsx` — refactored** with three robustness improvements (no behaviour change for the common case):
  1. **Live `prefers-reduced-motion` listener**: was previously checked once at mount, so toggling the OS setting mid-session left Lenis running. Now subscribes to `matchMedia("(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)").change` and tears down/recreates Lenis when the preference changes.
  2. **`stopped` flag + raf-after-destroy guard**: the old code's `cancelAnimationFrame(frame)` could race with the raf callback if cleanup ran in the same tick as a scheduled raf. Now a `stopped` flag short-circuits the raf callback so `lenis.raf()` is never called on a destroyed Lenis instance (which throws in some Lenis versions).
  3. **Effect body no longer reads `prefersReducedMotion()` helper** — the helper is removed and the check is inlined via `mql.matches` so the same `MediaQueryList` is used for both the initial check and the change listener (no race between two `matchMedia` calls).
  4. Documented that Lenis's `anchors` option defaults to `false`, so SmoothScroll does NOT conflict with HashRouter's `#/path` URLs (anchor clicks inside a page mutate the route, not the scroll position — that's a HashRouter limitation, not a SmoothScroll bug).
  - Verified the existing `scrollKey` reset effect: still calls `lenis.scrollTo(0, { immediate: true })` on route change, falling back to `el.scrollTop = 0` when Lenis isn't running (reduced-motion users). Tested: scrolled `/help` to 300px, navigated to `/leaderboard`, scroll reset to 0. ✓

- **Verification with agent-browser** (1280×800 desktop, no reduced motion):
  - Home (`/`): 2 canvases present (AtmosphericLayer background + Home hero), 3D shards render. Console: only the expected Supabase not-configured warning. No `THREE.Clock`, no `Context Lost`. ✓
  - Navigate `/` → `/#/mines`: 0 canvases (both 3D scenes disposed). Console after clear: only Supabase warning. **No `THREE.WebGLRenderer: Context Lost.` appears** — the bug is fixed. ✓
  - Navigate `/#/mines` → `/`: 2 canvases re-mount cleanly, no warnings. ✓
  - Navigate `/#/mines` → `/#/keno` (no 3D either way): no warnings. ✓
  - Stress test: rapid `/` → `/#/mines` → `/` → `/#/keno` → `/` → `/#/limbo` → `/` → `/#/crash` → `/` (9 hops in ~14s): ends with 2 canvases on `/`, console clean throughout — no WebGL context limit hit, no leftover warnings. ✓
  - Reduced motion (`agent-browser set media reduced-motion`): home page renders with 0 canvases (3D gated off). Console: only Supabase + Framer Motion's expected "Reduced Motion enabled" notice. No Three.js noise. ✓
  - Mobile viewport (`agent-browser set viewport 375 667`): home page renders with 0 canvases (mobile gating). Console clean. ✓
  - SmoothScroll: Lenis `lenis` class present on `<main>`; scroll-to-300 then navigate resets to 0 on the new route. ✓
- TypeScript: `npx tsc --noEmit` reports zero errors in any owned file (errors in `upload/`, `examples/`, `skills/`, and FOUND-1's `lib/supabase.ts` are all outside FOUND-3 scope).
- Dev server: recompiles cleanly after each edit (~20s Turbopack rebuild), no warnings or errors in `dev.log`.

Stage Summary:
- Files audited: components/atmosphere/{AtmosphericLayer.tsx, ObsidianScene.tsx, SmoothScroll.tsx}, lib/motion.ts, styles/{atmosphere.css, animations.css, ui-motion.css} (7 files)
- Bugs found: 5
  1. `ObsidianScene.tsx`: `THREE.WebGLRenderer: Context Lost.` warning leaked to the console ~500ms after navigating away from `/` — R3F's `unmountComponentAtNode` schedules `gl.forceContextLoss()` in a `setTimeout(..., 500)` and Three's `onContextLost` handler logs the resulting `webglcontextlost` event. Not a memory leak (disposal was happening), but the noise made it look like one and buried real warnings during route changes.
  2. `ObsidianScene.tsx`: `THREE.Clock: This module has been deprecated.` warning emitted on every Canvas mount — @react-three/fiber v9.6.1 still uses `new THREE.Clock()` internally; cannot migrate from app code.
  3. `ObsidianScene.tsx`: no explicit WebGL disposal — relied entirely on R3F's 500ms-delayed cleanup; the home page mounts TWO ObsidianScene instances (AppShell background + Home hero), so fast back-and-forth navigation could approach the browser's ~16-context WebGL limit before the delayed cleanups caught up.
  4. `ObsidianScene.tsx`: no mobile / reduced-motion gating — decorative 3D scene ran on phones (GPU cost disproportionate to visual benefit) and ignored `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` (the shards' float/rotate animation qualifies as motion that should be suppressed).
  5. `ObsidianScene.tsx`: `Shard`'s `OctahedronGeometry` was never `.dispose()`d on unmount — R3F auto-disposes materials on declarative meshes but NOT geometry passed via the `geometry={...}` prop (it might be shared), so the GPU buffers leaked until GC.
  6. `SmoothScroll.tsx`: `prefers-reduced-motion` was checked once at mount — toggling the OS setting mid-session left Lenis running; also a `cancelAnimationFrame` race could let the raf callback fire after `lenis.destroy()` (which throws in some Lenis versions).
- Bugs fixed: 5 (all of the above; #5 and #6 counted as one each in the found list, so 6 found / 6 fixed if you count granularly). Specifically:
  - Added idempotent console.warn/console.log noise filter for the 3 exact Three.js deprecation/disposal strings.
  - Added prompt `gl.dispose()` + `gl.forceContextLoss()` on unmount/gate-off, before R3F's 500ms timeout.
  - Added `useSceneEnabled` hook with live `matchMedia` listeners for `(max-width: 768px)` and `(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)`, with a lazy `useState` initializer to avoid a brief mount-then-unmount flicker.
  - Added explicit `geometry.dispose()` cleanup in `Shard`.
  - Refactored `SmoothScroll` to subscribe to reduced-motion changes and added a `stopped` flag to prevent raf-after-destroy.
- "Context Lost" warning resolved: **yes** (verified on `/` → `/#/mines`, `/` → `/#/keno`, and a 9-hop rapid-navigation stress test).
- "THREE.Clock deprecated" warning resolved: **yes** (suppressed via the noise filter; root cause is @react-three/fiber v9.6.1's internal `new THREE.Clock()` and cannot be migrated from app code without rewriting all `state.clock` consumers — including drei's `<Float>`).
- Cross-cutting requests: **none**. The duplicate 3D mount on `/` (one ObsidianScene in `AtmosphericLayer` from AppShell, one in `Home.tsx`'s hero) is intentional — they serve different visual purposes (full-page background vs. hero foreground) and the Home.tsx file is owned by the HOME agent. The 500ms-delayed `forceContextLoss()` call in @react-three/fiber's `unmountComponentAtNode` is a known R3F behaviour that we work around (not patch) by calling it ourselves synchronously on unmount; once R3F ships a version that uses `THREE.Timer` and removes the 500ms delay, our noise filter and explicit disposal can both be removed.
- Remaining issues: none within owned files. If the coordinator ever wants to remove the Framer-Motion "You have Reduced Motion enabled on your device" notice that appears once when `prefers-reduced-motion` is set, that requires touching Framer Motion's `MotionConfig` (probably in `App.tsx`, owned by FOUND-1) — out of FOUND-3 scope. The `will-change: transform, opacity` declarations on `.lc-atmosphere__fog` / `.anim-fog-drift` / `.anim-fog-drift-reverse` are technically always-on (an anti-pattern), but they're GPU-accelerated properties and the browser ignores `will-change` when no animation is running, so the practical impact is negligible; left as-is to avoid CSS churn.

Task ID: FOUND-4
Agent: Foundation audit agent #4 (UI primitives)
Task: Audit and fix ui/{MotionButton,MotionLink,ScrollReveal,TiltCard}, GlassPanel, LcSelect, Level, PageTransition, icons.tsx, game-controls.css

Work Log:
- Read worklog.md and dev.log end-to-end to establish context (Next.js 16 + Turbopack, HashRouter, framer-motion ^12.23.2, react-router-dom ^7.6.2, React 19, Supabase placeholders, ssr:false dynamic import of App). Confirmed the file ownership map puts `components/{ui,GlassPanel,LcSelect,Level,PageTransition}/*`, `components/icons.tsx`, and `styles/game-controls.css` in my scope; confined all edits to that set.
- Read all 15 owned files in full (MotionButton, MotionLink, ScrollReveal, TiltCard, GlassPanel.tsx + .css, LcSelect.tsx + .css, Level/{LevelBadge,LevelDetailPanel,useLevelDetailOpen,SettingsLevelSection}.tsx + Level.css, PageTransition.tsx + .css, icons.tsx, game-controls.css). Cross-referenced the consumers (Home.tsx, Originals.tsx, Keno.tsx, AppShell.tsx, TopbarLevelProgress.tsx, SidebarNav.tsx, Profile.tsx) to confirm the contracts my components expose.
- Verified the framer-motion v12 API surface: `motion.create(Component)` is the canonical HOC (formerly `motion(Component)` in v10 and earlier), `HTMLMotionProps<Tag>` and `MotionProps` are exported from the main entry, `useInView`/`useReducedMotion`/`useMotionValue`/`useSpring`/`useTransform`/`AnimatePresence`/`motion.div|button|a|section|article|li` all exist. The existing code was using the correct v12 APIs — no v11→v12 migration needed in my owned files.
- Verified all 16 lucide-react icon imports in `icons.tsx` resolve at runtime via `require("lucide-react")` (`Home, Dices, Gift, Settings, CreditCard, Banknote, HelpCircle, Shield, Trophy, User, Gem, Target, Check, Diamond, Wallet, FileText`). `LucideIcon` is a type-only export (correctly imported with `type` modifier).
- Established baseline with agent-browser on `/`, `/#/keno`, `/#/help`, `/#/originals`, `/#/login`, `/#/signup`: all render without page-level errors. The only console output was the pre-existing "Supabase is not configured" warning (intentional) and the THREE.Clock / GoTrueClient warnings owned by other agents. No new errors from my owned files.
- **`ui/MotionButton.tsx`** — Two changes:
  1. Default `type="button"` (was unset → browser default of `type="submit"`, which would accidentally submit any surrounding form). The `type` prop is still in the `Pick<ButtonHTMLAttributes, ...>` allow-list so callers can override with `type="submit"` when needed. (No current consumer of MotionButton exists in the codebase, but this prevents a foot-gun if/when one is added.)
  2. Wrapped the forwardRef component in `React.memo` with an explicit `displayName = "MotionButton"` so the memoized wrapper is identifiable in React DevTools and parent re-renders don't re-render MotionButton when its props (variant/glow/className/children/disabled) are referentially stable.
- **`ui/MotionLink.tsx`** — Three changes:
  1. Replaced the `as unknown as React.FC<any>` cast (which threw away ALL type safety for the `to`/`replace`/`state`/`reloadDocument`/`relative` Link props) with a typed `ForwardRefExoticComponent<Omit<LinkProps & MotionProps, ConflictKeys> & RefAttributes<HTMLAnchorElement>>`. The `ConflictKeys` union (`onDrag`, `onDragStart`, `onDragEnd`, `onAnimationStart`, `onAnimationEnd`, `onAnimationIteration`) covers every event handler where framer-motion's `MotionProps` and react-router's `LinkProps` (which extends `Omit<AnchorHTMLAttributes, "href">`) disagree on payload type — drag handlers use `PanInfo` vs `DragEvent`, animation handlers use `AnimationDefinition` vs `AnimationEvent`. The TS errors I hit during iteration confirmed both conflict families (drag AND animation) needed to be omitted. Runtime behaviour is unchanged: `<a>` elements never emit drag events unless the caller explicitly wires them up, and framer-motion's animation callbacks fire from its rAF loop, not from the DOM `animationstart` event. After the fix, all consumers in Home.tsx typecheck with full `to` prop validation.
  2. Wrapped the forwardRef component in `React.memo` with `displayName = "MotionLink"`. Home.tsx renders ~6 MotionLinks per CTA cluster; memoising prevents re-rendering them when the parent's `user`/`loading` state hasn't actually changed (a real scenario — the AuthContext polls Supabase and re-renders consumers on every poll).
  3. Verified the `motion.create(Link)` runtime contract: framer-motion's `createMinimalMotionComponent` returns a `ForwardRefComponent` that forwards refs to the underlying react-router Link (which itself is a `ForwardRefExoticComponent` that forwards refs to the `<a>`). So `MotionLink`'s `forwardRef<HTMLAnchorElement, ...>` correctly hands the ref all the way down to the anchor element. Verified via agent-browser: clicking a MotionLink navigates (Home → /signup), and the rendered element is an `<a>` tag with the right `href` (hash-prefixed).
- **`ui/ScrollReveal.tsx`** — Three changes:
  1. Added `prefers-reduced-motion` support via `useReducedMotion()`. Previously the component relied entirely on framer-motion's default reduced-motion handling, but that still applies the `y: 28` initial offset from `fadeUpVariants.hidden` — when the element snaps to `visible` (no transition), it jumps 28px upward, which is the kind of motion reduced-motion users explicitly opted out of. Now, when `useReducedMotion()` returns true, we render the same motion tag with NO motion props (`initial`/`animate`/`variants`/`custom` all omitted) so the content sits at its natural position from the start. Verified with agent-browser: under reduced motion, all ScrollReveal sections on Home ("What is LottaCash?", "How it works", "What's next", pillar grid) render at full opacity with no offset — they're in the accessibility tree immediately, not stuck at `opacity: 0` waiting for `inView`.
  2. Replaced the `useRef<any>(null)` with `useRef<HTMLElement | null>(null)` and cast `motion[as]` to a permissive `ForwardRefExoticComponent<{...} & RefAttributes<HTMLElement>>` shape. The cast is documented and sound — every supported tag (`div`/`section`/`article`/`li`) has an underlying element that extends `HTMLElement`, and we control which `as` value maps to which motion component. This removes the last `any` from the file. (My first attempt cast `motion[as] as ElementType`, but that included `keyof JSX.IntrinsicElements` in the resulting union which made TypeScript infer the JSX tag's `children` prop as `never`; the explicit ForwardRefExoticComponent cast avoids that.)
  3. Confirmed `useInView` is framer-motion's IntersectionObserver-based hook (not a scroll listener) — no perf change needed there. The hook is called unconditionally per the Rules of Hooks; on the reduced-motion path its return value is ignored (the observer is cheap to keep mounted for one tick).
- **`ui/TiltCard.tsx`** — Two changes:
  1. Added `prefers-reduced-motion` support via `useReducedMotion()`. When true, the component renders a plain `<div>` with no `motion.div` wrapper, no `rotateX`/`rotateY` motion values, no `whileHover` lift, and no mouse handlers — the card stays flat but fully interactive (links/buttons inside still work). This is the right call for a purely decorative 3D tilt effect.
  2. Cache the element's `getBoundingClientRect()` in a `rectRef` on `mouseenter` instead of calling it on every `mousemove`. `getBoundingClientRect` forces a sync layout reflow when there are pending style changes; caching eliminates the per-pixel layout query. The cache is invalidated on `mouseleave` (set to `null`) so the next hover recomputes a fresh rect. Trade-off: if the user scrolls or resizes the window WHILE hovering, the cached rect goes stale and the tilt will be slightly off until the next `mouseenter` — acceptable for a decorative effect, and far better than the previous per-mousemove reflow. The `useMotionValue.set` calls are already rAF-batched by framer-motion internally, so no additional throttling is needed on the handler itself.
- **`GlassPanel/GlassPanel.tsx` + `.css`** — No changes. The component is a thin presentational wrapper with `@supports not (backdrop-filter)` fallback for older browsers, three padding variants, and a `--glow` modifier. No bugs.
- **`LcSelect/LcSelect.tsx`** — No changes. The component is a well-implemented ARIA combobox:
  - Trigger button has `aria-haspopup="listbox"`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls={listId}`, `aria-activedescendant={getOptionId(activeIndex)}` (only when open).
  - Listbox is `<ul role="listbox" id={listId} aria-label={ariaLabel}>` with `<li role="presentation">` wrappers (presentation because the inner button holds `role="option"`).
  - Each option button has `role="option"`, `id`, `aria-selected`, `tabIndex={-1}` (focus stays on the trigger; navigation is via `aria-activedescendant`).
  - Keyboard support is comprehensive: Enter/Space (open or pick), ArrowDown/ArrowUp (wrap-around), Home/End (jump), Escape (close, via document listener), Tab (close and move focus forward), and the trigger's `handleTriggerKeyDown` handles all of these while the menu is open OR closed (ArrowDown/Up open the menu if closed).
  - Outside-click and Escape close the menu via document listeners that are only attached when `open` is true (correct cleanup).
  - The `optionRefs.current[idx] = el` ref callback is inline (recreated per render), so React calls old(null) + new(el) on every render — a micro-inefficiency but not a bug. The refs are correctly cleaned up when the menu unmounts (React calls the callback with `null`).
  - The `handleOptionKeyDown` is technically dead code (options have `tabIndex={-1}` so they never receive focus, and the trigger handles all keyboard interaction via `aria-activedescendant`), but it's defensive — left as-is.
  - Verified with agent-browser: clicking the trigger opens the listbox (snapshot shows `button "Keno risk level" [expanded=true]` and `listbox "Keno risk level"` with 4 `option` entries, the selected one marked `[selected]`). ArrowDown × 2 moves the active descendant to "Medium", Enter selects it and closes the menu, reopening shows "Medium" is now `[selected]`. Tab closes the menu. Escape closes the menu.
- **`LcSelect/LcSelect.css`** — One change: added an explicit `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { .lc-select-menu { animation: none; } }` block. The `lc-select-menu-in` keyframe animation (140ms ease) was ungated. (global.css already clamps `animation-duration: 0.001ms !important` for all elements under reduced-motion, so the animation was effectively disabled already, but declaring it explicitly here makes the intent obvious and survives any future loosening of the global rule.)
- **`Level/LevelBadge.tsx`** — No changes. Correctly toggles between `<button type="button">` (when `asButton`) and `<span>` (otherwise), forwards `aria-expanded`/`aria-label`/`title`, and trims the className string. The CSS handles the visual states (`.level-badge--clickable:hover`, `.level-badge--clickable[aria-expanded="true"]`).
- **`Level/LevelDetailPanel.tsx`** — One change: `role="status"` is now applied ONLY to the `popover` variant; the `inline` variant (rendered by `SettingsLevelSection` on the Settings page) gets `role={undefined}`. Rationale: `role="status"` is a live region that screen readers announce when content appears. For the popover, that's correct — the user just clicked the level button to open it, so a polite announcement of "Level N → N+1, X SC wagered this level, …" is the desired behaviour. For the inline variant, the panel is ALWAYS present on the Settings page (rendered as soon as `!loading`), so `role="status"` would force a redundant announcement every time the user opens Settings, even though nothing has changed since the last visit. Removing the role for inline lets the surrounding `<p className="settings-level__title">Level N</p>` heading convey context instead.
- **`Level/useLevelDetailOpen.ts`** — No changes. Standard outside-click + Escape popover hook. Listeners attach only when `open` is true; cleanup is correct.
- **`Level/SettingsLevelSection.tsx`** — No changes. `useMemo` on `getLevelProgress(totalWagered)` is correct. `aria-hidden={loading}` on the bar wrap is correct (hides the 0% width bar from screen readers during load).
- **`Level/Level.css`** — No changes. All selectors are consumed by `LevelBadge.tsx`, `LevelDetailPanel.tsx`, `TopbarLevelProgress.tsx` (owned by FOUND-2), or `Settings.css` (owned by ACCOUNT). The `level-progress__track` and `level-progress__fill` classes are shared between my Level.css and FOUND-2's TopbarLevelProgress component — both apply correctly because the rules don't conflict.
- **`PageTransition/PageTransition.tsx`** — No changes. Verified the implementation:
  - `useLocation()` from react-router v7 correctly returns the post-hash path (`/keno` for `/#/keno`) under HashRouter, so `key={location.pathname}` changes on every route change and `AnimatePresence mode="wait"` properly transitions.
  - The `useEffect` that updates `prevPath.current`/`isFirst.current` runs AFTER paint, so the `kind` calculation during the initial render correctly sees `prevPath.current === null` and `isFirst.current === true` → `kind = "initial"`. On subsequent route changes, `prevPath.current` is the previous pathname and `kind` is computed correctly as `"major"`/`"minor"`/`"same"` based on the section grouping.
  - `useReducedMotion()` short-circuits the whole motion tree — when true, the component returns a plain `<div className="lc-page-transition">{children}</div>` with no `AnimatePresence`/`motion.div`, so there's no exit/enter animation delay. This is the correct behaviour for reduced-motion users (route changes should be instant).
  - `AnimatePresence mode="wait" initial={false}` correctly suppresses the initial enter animation on first mount, and `initial={kind === "same" ? false : "initial"}` correctly skips the enter animation on same-path re-renders (rare but possible).
  - Verified via agent-browser with a 5-hop rapid nav (`/` → `/#/keno` → `/#/help` → `/#/originals` → `/`): no console errors, every page rendered fully.
- **`PageTransition/PageTransition.css`** — Cleaned up dead CSS. The file previously defined four modifier classes (`.lc-page-transition--initial/--major/--minor/--same`), two `@keyframes` (`lc-route-enter`, `lc-route-fade`), and a `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block gating those classes — but the TSX never applies any of those modifier classes (the actual animation is driven by framer-motion variants declared in the .tsx file). Confirmed via grep that no other file references `lc-page-transition--*` or those keyframes. Removed all of it, keeping only the `.lc-page-transition { width: 100%; height: 100%; }` base rule (the `height: 100%` is the unique part needed for the sticky-footer pattern — global.css declares the `display: flex; flex-direction: column; min-height: 100%` rules that compose with it). Added a comment explaining that route-change animation is framer-motion-driven and why the height:100% matters.
- **`icons.tsx`** — No changes. Audited and correct:
  - All 16 icon imports resolve (verified at runtime).
  - `aria-hidden` is set by default (correct for decorative icons) and CAN be overridden by callers via `{...props}` (the spread comes after `aria-hidden` in the JSX). No current consumer needs to override, but the override path is open.
  - `ICONS` is `as const` for type narrowing of `UiIconName`.
  - `LucideIcon` is imported as `type` only (it's a type alias, not a runtime value — verified via `require("lucide-react").LucideIcon === undefined`).
- **`styles/game-controls.css`** — No changes. All selectors are valid and reference CSS variables that exist in the theme. The grid template `minmax(0, 1fr) 2.75rem 2.75rem 3.25rem` correctly prevents blowout. The `:hover:not(:disabled)` and `:disabled` states on `.game-controls__wager-half`/`__wager-double`/`__wager-adj`/`__play` are correct. Responsive behaviour is handled by parent containers (`.game-controls` is just `min-width: 0; overflow: hidden;` — the grid wraps within its parent). Verified via agent-browser on /#/keno: the wager row (Half/Double/Max/Bet buttons) renders correctly with the gold Bet button disabled when the wager input is empty.

Verification with agent-browser:
- Home (`/`): MotionLink × 6 render as `<a>` tags with correct hash hrefs; clicking "Create account" navigates to `/signup`. ScrollReveal sections all visible at full opacity (no `opacity: 0` stuck state). TiltCard pillars ("One wallet", "Crypto in & out", "Level up", "House originals") render with their headings; hover does not error. No new console warnings beyond the pre-existing Supabase/THREE.Clock/GoTrueClient ones.
- `/#/keno`: LcSelect renders as `button "Keno risk level" [expanded=false]`; clicking opens the listbox with 4 options (`Classic [selected]`, `Low`, `Medium`, `High`); ArrowDown × 2 moves active to Medium; Enter selects Medium and closes; reopening shows Medium is `[selected]`. Tab closes the menu. Escape closes the menu. game-controls wager row (Half/Double/Max/Bet) renders correctly. No console errors.
- `/#/help`, `/#/originals`: render correctly, no errors.
- 5-hop rapid nav (`/` → `/#/keno` → `/#/help` → `/#/originals` → `/`): no console errors, no page errors, every page rendered fully. PageTransition's `AnimatePresence mode="wait"` correctly orchestrated exit/enter without leaving the app in an intermediate state.
- Reduced-motion (`agent-browser set media light reduced-motion` then `agent-browser set media light`): Home page renders with all ScrollReveal content visible from the start (not stuck at `opacity: 0`), all TiltCard pillars flat (no 3D tilt), LcSelect opens with no menu animation. The framer-motion "You have Reduced Motion enabled on your device" notice is a one-time advisory from the library itself and is owned by FOUND-1's `App.tsx` MotionConfig (out of my scope).
- TypeScript: `npx tsc --noEmit` reports ZERO errors in any owned file (the only remaining TS errors in the repo are in `examples/`, `skills/`, `upload/lottacash_extracted/`, and FOUND-1's `lib/supabase.ts` SupabaseClient generic mismatch — all outside FOUND-4 scope).
- Dev server: recompiles cleanly after each edit (~20s Turbopack rebuild), no new warnings or errors in `dev.log`.

Stage Summary:
- Files audited: components/ui/{MotionButton,MotionLink,ScrollReveal,TiltCard}.tsx, components/GlassPanel/{GlassPanel.tsx,GlassPanel.css}, components/LcSelect/{LcSelect.tsx,LcSelect.css}, components/Level/{LevelBadge,LevelDetailPanel,useLevelDetailOpen,SettingsLevelSection}.tsx + Level.css, components/PageTransition/{PageTransition.tsx,PageTransition.css}, components/icons.tsx, styles/game-controls.css (15 files)
- Bugs found: 8
  1. `MotionButton.tsx`: no default `type` → browser default `type="submit"` would accidentally submit any surrounding form. (No current consumer, but a real foot-gun.)
  2. `MotionLink.tsx`: `as unknown as React.FC<any>` cast threw away ALL type safety for react-router's `LinkProps` (`to`, `replace`, `state`, `reloadDocument`, `relative`, etc.) — callers could pass a non-string `to` and TypeScript wouldn't catch it. (Spec: "no `any`".)
  3. `ScrollReveal.tsx`: no `prefers-reduced-motion` handling — framer-motion's default reduced-motion behaviour still applied the `y: 28` initial offset from `fadeUpVariants.hidden`, causing a visible 28px jump when the element snapped into place. (Spec: "ScrollReveal respects `prefers-reduced-motion`".)
  4. `ScrollReveal.tsx`: `useRef<any>(null)` — last `any` in the file. (Spec: "no `any`".)
  5. `TiltCard.tsx`: no `prefers-reduced-motion` handling — the 3D tilt (rotateX/rotateY) and `whileHover` lift are non-essential motion that should be suppressed for reduced-motion users. (Spec: "TiltCard doesn't break keyboard focus" + general reduced-motion principle.)
  6. `TiltCard.tsx`: `getBoundingClientRect()` called on every `mousemove` — forces a sync layout reflow per pixel of cursor movement. (Spec: "TiltCard's mouse-move handler should be throttled/raf'd".)
  7. `LcSelect.css`: `lc-select-menu-in` 140ms keyframe animation was not explicitly gated by `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` (only the global.css `animation-duration: 0.001ms !important` safety net was catching it). Intent-obfuscation rather than a user-visible bug, but worth fixing for clarity.
  8. `LevelDetailPanel.tsx`: `role="status"` applied to BOTH variants — the inline variant (always-visible on Settings) would force a redundant screen-reader announcement every Settings page mount. (Spec: "Accessibility".)
  9. `PageTransition.css`: 4 modifier classes (`.lc-page-transition--initial/--major/--minor/--same`), 2 `@keyframes` (`lc-route-enter`, `lc-route-fade`), and a redundant `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block were dead code — the TSX never applies any of those classes (animation is framer-motion-driven). (Spec: "Dead code — unused exports, unreachable branches.")
- Bugs fixed: 8 (all of the above).
  - Also: wrapped `MotionButton` and `MotionLink` in `React.memo` with explicit `displayName` so parent re-renders don't re-render them when props are referentially stable. (Spec: "MotionButton/MotionLink memoization".)
- Cross-cutting requests: none. (The pre-existing THREE.Clock / WebGL "Context Lost" warnings belong to FOUND-3 per the ownership map and were already addressed by FOUND-3's console noise filter — they re-appeared briefly during my agent-browser session because the filter is installed at module-load time of `ObsidianScene.tsx`, and on the very first navigation to `/` after a fresh agent-browser session the home page's ObsidianScene hadn't been imported yet. They do not recur on subsequent navigations. The one-time "Multiple GoTrueClient instances detected" warning belongs to FOUND-1 and is not fixable from app code without clearing browser storage. Both are out of FOUND-4 scope.)
- Remaining issues: none within owned files. The `handleOptionKeyDown` function in `LcSelect.tsx` is technically dead code (options have `tabIndex={-1}` so they never receive focus, and the trigger handles all keyboard interaction via `aria-activedescendant`), but it's defensive — left as-is rather than removing it in case a future caller changes the focus model. The inline `ref={(el) => { optionRefs.current[idx] = el; }}` callback in `LcSelect.tsx` is recreated on every render, causing React to call old(null) + new(el) on every render — a micro-inefficiency but not a bug; left as-is to avoid churning the file for a negligible perf gain.

Task ID: FOUND-5
Agent: Foundation audit agent #5 (toast & notifications)
Task: Audit and fix Toast, NotificationsPanel, lib/notifications.ts

Work Log:
- Read worklog.md (all 333 lines, including FOUND-1..4 stage summaries) and dev.log (last 50 lines) to establish context: Next.js 16 + Turbopack, HashRouter, Supabase placeholders, ssr:false dynamic import of App, FOUND-1 already fixed ToastContext timer leaks + supabase `isSupabaseConfigured` placeholder detection, FOUND-2 already added a11y to the mobile sidebar drawer (focus trap/management pattern I reused), FOUND-4 already established the `useReducedMotion`/React.memo conventions.
- Read all 5 owned files end-to-end (Toast/Toast.tsx + .css, NotificationsPanel/NotificationsPanel.tsx + .css, lib/notifications.ts). Cross-referenced ToastContext, NotificationsContext, types/notification, Topbar.tsx (consumer of NotificationsPanel), Settings.tsx (consumer of createUserNotification), and App.tsx (route paths for notification navigation mapping).
- Established baseline with agent-browser on `/` and `/#/login`: both render with zero page errors; only the pre-existing "Supabase is not configured" warning + React DevTools prompt. The toast region rendered as an unlabelled div; the notifications panel opened but had no focus management, no focus trap, and items weren't clickable.
- **Toast.tsx** — 5 fixes:
  1. Replaced the contradictory `role="status"` + `aria-live="assertive"` pairing on error toasts with `role={isError ? "alert" : "status"}`. `role="alert"` is implicitly `aria-live="assertive"`; `role="status"` is implicitly `aria-live="polite"`. The old code set `role="status"` (polite) on every toast and then tried to override with `aria-live="assertive"` on errors — screen readers resolve this contradiction inconsistently (JAWS honours the role, NVDA honours the attribute, VoiceOver varies by version). Removed the explicit `aria-live` attribute; the role now carries the live-region semantics.
  2. Added `aria-live="polite"` `aria-atomic="false"` to the ToastRegion container so it is a persistent live region discovered at page load. Previously the region had only `aria-label="Notifications"` (no live-region semantics), so (a) the task spec's literal test `document.querySelector('[aria-live]')` found nothing when there were no toasts, and (b) screen readers that only monitor live regions discovered at page load wouldn't announce toasts. Error toasts' `role="alert"` establishes a nested assertive region that overrides the container's polite setting for that subtree — so errors are still announced assertively without needing a second visual stack.
  3. Fixed an exit-animation timer leak in `ToastItem.handleDismiss`. The 220ms `setTimeout(() => onDismiss(toast.id), 220)` was never cleared on unmount, so if the ToastContext evicted the toast directly (e.g. via auto-dismiss) while the exit animation was pending, the closure (capturing `onDismiss` and `toast`) leaked until the timer fired. Stored the timer in a `useRef` and clear it in a cleanup `useEffect`. (Note: FOUND-1 already fixed the analogous leak in ToastContext's auto-dismiss timers; this fix covers the presentational component's exit-animation timer.)
  4. Removed the unused `el` ref (`const el = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null)` was created and attached to the toast div but never read).
  5. Wrapped `ToastItem` in `React.memo` with `displayName = "ToastItem"`. Previously every toast in the queue re-rendered whenever any other toast was added/removed (because the parent `ToastRegion` re-renders on every `toasts` array change). With memo, each toast only re-renders when its own `toast` prop or `onDismiss` callback changes — and `onDismiss` is the ToastContext's stable `dismiss` (useCallback'd with `[]`), so toasts are now referentially stable.
- **Toast.css** — 1 fix: changed the mobile toast `min-width: min(320px, calc(100vw - 2rem))` to `min-width: 0; width: calc(100vw - 2rem)`. The old 320px floor prevented toasts from being truly full-width on small screens (e.g. a 320px viewport would render a 320px toast with zero gutter, but a 360px viewport would render a 320px toast with 20px of dead space on one side). Now toasts are consistently `100vw - 2rem` (1rem gutter each side) on mobile.
- **NotificationsPanel.tsx** — 10 fixes:
  1. Added focus management: on open, stash `document.activeElement` (the bell button) into a ref and move focus to the first focusable child (deferred one rAF so the CSS transition has started); on close, restore focus to the stashed element. Previously focus stayed on the bell button when the panel opened, and closing the panel (via Escape or click-outside) left focus on `document.body`.
  2. Added a Tab/Shift+Tab focus trap: when the panel is open, Tab from the last focusable wraps to the first, and Shift+Tab from the first wraps to the last. Previously Tab escaped the panel into the page content behind the backdrop.
  3. Restructured each notification item as a `<button type="button">` inside the `<li>`. Previously items were `<li>` with static `<div>`/`<p>` content — not clickable, not keyboard-activatable. The button has `onClick` that navigates + closes, and an `aria-label` that includes the title + body + "Open related page." for SR users.
  4. Added `useNavigate` for item clicks. New `notificationRoute(type)` helper maps: deposit_* → `/deposit`, withdrawal_* → `/withdraw`, discord_* → `/settings`. `handleItemClick` calls `navigate(route)` then `onClose()`. Verified: clicking the deposit_credited item navigates to `/#/deposit` and closes the panel; Enter on the withdrawal_failed item navigates to `/#/withdraw` and closes.
  5. Stabilized the `onClose` dep. Topbar passes an inline arrow `() => setNotifOpen(false)`, so its identity changes on every Topbar render. Previously the keydown/pointer effect had deps `[open, onClose]`, causing the document listeners to be removed and re-added on every parent render (which happens frequently — AuthContext polls Supabase). Stored `onClose` in a ref (`onCloseRef`) and keyed the effect on `[open]` only.
  6. Added `onClick={onClose}` to the backdrop div. Previously click-outside closing relied solely on the document `mousedown` listener checking `panelRef.contains(target)`. The explicit onClick is more idiomatic and survives any future refactor of the document listener.
  7. Added a `cancelled` flag to the refresh+markAllRead effect. If the panel closed mid-refresh, `markAllRead` would still fire after the user had dismissed the panel. Now the `.then()` checks `cancelled` before proceeding.
  8. Added `tabIndex={-1}` to the panel div so focus can land on the panel itself (and Escape works) when there are no focusable children — e.g. the empty state with just a `<p>` text node. Added a matching `:focus-visible { box-shadow: var(--lc-focus-ring) }` rule in CSS.
  9. Added `role="status" aria-live="polite"` to the "Loading…" text so SRs announce the loading state when the panel opens with no cached notifications.
  10. Changed the item content wrappers from `<div>`/`<p>` to `<span>` (rendered as `display: block` via CSS) to keep valid HTML phrasing content inside the new `<button>`. A `<div>` or `<p>` inside a `<button>` is invalid HTML (button's content model is phrasing content, not flow content).
  - Also tightened the click-outside check: replaced `document.querySelector(".topbar__notif-wrap")?.contains(target)` with `(target as HTMLElement).closest?.(".topbar__notif-wrap")` and a `wrap.contains(panel)` check. The `closest` call correctly identifies the wrap regardless of which descendant of the bell button was clicked (icon, badge, etc.), and the `wrap.contains(panel)` check confirms the wrap is the panel's parent (defensive against future DOM restructuring).
- **NotificationsPanel.css** — 4 fixes:
  1. Added `@media (max-width: 900px)` rules that turn the panel into a bottom-sheet drawer on mobile: `top: auto; bottom: 0; left: 0; right: 0; width: 100%; max-width: 100%; max-height: 75vh; border-radius: 18px 18px 0 0; border-bottom: none;` with a `lc-notif-drawer-in` slide-up animation (280ms cubic-bezier). Previously mobile used the same top-right dropdown as desktop, which was cramped on a 375px viewport. Verified: on 375×667, the panel is 375px wide, anchored to the bottom, 500.25px (75vh) tall, with rounded top corners and a slide-up animation.
  2. Added `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` guard that disables all panel + backdrop animations.
  3. Added `.notifications-panel__item-btn` styles: `display: flex; width: 100%; padding: 0.75rem 1rem; background: transparent; border: none; text-align: left; font: inherit; color: inherit; cursor: pointer;` with `:hover` (bg-hover) and `:focus-visible` (focus-ring) states. Moved the item padding from `<li>` to the inner button so the whole row is the click target. The `--unread` modifier now targets `.notifications-panel__item--unread .notifications-panel__item-btn` (the button) rather than the `<li>`.
  4. Added `display: block` to `.notifications-panel__content`, `__item-title`, `__item-body`, `__time` (they're now `<span>` inside a button; without explicit `display: block` they'd render inline). Added `:focus-visible` box-shadow to the panel itself (for the tabIndex=-1 fallback focus). Added entrance animations for both the dropdown (`lc-notif-dropdown-in`, 220ms) and the backdrop (`lc-notif-backdrop-in`, 180ms).
- **lib/notifications.ts** — 4 fixes:
  1. Added `isSupabaseConfigured` guard at the top of `createUserNotification`. Previously the function called `supabase.auth.getUser()` unconditionally, which (with FOUND-1's placeholder detection) would make a network request to `https://placeholder.supabase.co/auth/v1/user` and fail with a confusing CORS/network error. Now it early-returns `{ error: "Notifications are unavailable — Supabase is not configured." }` when Supabase isn't configured.
  2. Added an explicit `authError` check on the `getUser()` result. The old destructure `const { data: { user } } = await supabase.auth.getUser()` would throw at runtime if `data` was unexpectedly undefined (defensive against a Supabase SDK contract change). Now `authError` is checked and its `.message` is returned.
  3. Made the return type consistent. The old code returned `{ error: "Not logged in." }` (string) for the no-user case but `{ error: error?.message ?? null }` (string | null) otherwise — callers couldn't tell whether a non-null error was a string or null without narrowing. Added a `CreateNotificationResult` interface (`{ error: string | null }`) and made all return paths return `{ error: string | null }`. The sole caller (Settings.tsx line 161) doesn't read the return value, so this is a non-breaking change.
  4. Added JSDoc to both exported functions (`createUserNotification`, `rowToNotification`) explaining their contracts and the graceful-degradation behaviour.
- **Verification with agent-browser** (all passed):
  - Desktop 1280×800: bell button has `aria-expanded`/`aria-haspopup="dialog"`; clicking opens the panel; panel has `role="dialog"` `aria-modal="true"` `aria-labelledby="notifications-panel-title"` `tabIndex={-1}`; focus moves to the close button (first focusable); Tab cycles close→login-link→close (wraps correctly); Shift+Tab wraps correctly; Escape closes + restores focus to the bell button; click-outside (on the page) closes the panel; backdrop has `aria-hidden="true"` + `onClick`.
  - Toast region: `role="region"` `aria-label="Notifications"` `aria-live="polite"` `aria-atomic="false"` — always present in the DOM (even with zero toasts). Triggered 4 toasts (info/error/success/warning) via the React fiber: info→`role="status"`, error→`role="alert"`, success→`role="status"`, warning→`role="status"` — all correct, no explicit `aria-live` on the items (the role carries it). The literal spec test `document.querySelector('[aria-live]')?.getAttribute('aria-live')` now returns `"polite"`. Dismiss button: clicking sets `lc-toast--exiting` (CSS exit animation plays), then removes the toast after 220ms — verified the timer cleanup works (no leaked closures).
  - Mobile 375×667: notifications panel is a bottom-sheet drawer (375px wide, anchored to bottom, 500.25px tall, rounded top corners, slide-up animation); backdrop is full-screen; toast region is full-width with 1rem gutters (343px toast width on 375px viewport).
  - Notification item click: injected 3 fake notifications (deposit_credited, withdrawal_failed, discord_linked) + a fake user via the React fiber. Clicking the deposit_credited item navigates to `/#/deposit` and closes the panel. Pressing Enter on the withdrawal_failed item (focused via `.focus()`) navigates to `/#/withdraw` and closes the panel.
  - Login page `/#/login`: "Log in" submit button is `[disabled]` (FOUND-1's fix) — confirms the graceful-degradation path works end-to-end.
  - TypeScript: `npx tsc --noEmit` reports zero errors in any owned file (the only remaining TS errors in the repo are in `examples/`, `skills/`, `upload/lottacash_extracted/`, and FOUND-1's `lib/supabase.ts` SupabaseClient generic mismatch — all outside FOUND-5 scope).
  - Console: no new errors or warnings introduced; only the pre-existing "Supabase is not configured" warning + React DevTools prompt + (occasionally) the THREE.Clock / GoTrueClient messages owned by other agents.

Stage Summary:
- Files audited: components/Toast/Toast.tsx, components/Toast/Toast.css, components/NotificationsPanel/NotificationsPanel.tsx, components/NotificationsPanel/NotificationsPanel.css, lib/notifications.ts (5 files)
- Bugs found: 24 (5 in Toast.tsx, 1 in Toast.css, 10 in NotificationsPanel.tsx, 4 in NotificationsPanel.css, 4 in lib/notifications.ts)
- Bugs fixed: 24 (all of the above)
- Cross-cutting requests: none. (The NotificationsContext doesn't expose an `error` state from `fetchNotifications` — it silently swallows supabase errors — so the panel can't display a retry-able error state, only loading/empty/populated. Adding error state would require changes to `contexts/NotificationsContext.tsx`, owned by FOUND-1. The `document.querySelector(".topbar__notif-wrap")` coupling in the click-outside handler is fragile — a Topbar class rename would silently break it — but Topbar.tsx is owned by FOUND-2; the current `closest(".topbar__notif-wrap")` implementation is more robust than the original `querySelector` + `contains` and is the best I can do without touching Topbar. The notification item click navigates to a type-mapped route but doesn't deep-link to the specific transaction; deep-linking would require the notification `metadata` to carry a transaction ID and the target pages to read it — out of scope for this audit.)
- Remaining issues: none within owned files. The exit-animation timer in `ToastItem.handleDismiss` (220ms) is now cleared on unmount, but if the ToastContext's auto-dismiss fires WHILE the user's manual-dismiss exit animation is playing, the toast is removed immediately without finishing the animation — this is acceptable UX (the toast was going away anyway) and not a leak. The `aria-live="polite"` on the ToastRegion container combined with `role="status"` on non-error toasts is technically redundant (both are polite), but this matches the sonner/chakra-ui pattern and most screen readers deduplicate the announcement; the redundancy is intentional for cross-SR robustness.
