I left split as-is; flagging the discrepancy.
- **lib/roulette.ts — 1 fix** (type safety):
  1. `placeRouletteBet` and `setRouletteClientSeed` had implicit return types. Made both explicit (`Promise<{ data: RouletteBetResult | null; error: string | null }>` and `Promise<{ error: string | null }>`) so consumers get full type-checking on the data path. (Behaviour unchanged; same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH bugs #1, #2 for `lib/limbo.ts`/`lib/crash.ts`.)
- **lib/blackjack.ts — 2 fixes** (type safety + defensive parsing):
  1. All 9 exported functions (`fetchBlackjackPfState`, `setBlackjackClientSeed`, `blackjackAction`, `startBlackjack`, `hitBlackjack`, `standBlackjack`, `doubleBlackjack`, `splitBlackjack`, `insuranceBlackjack`, `fetchActiveBlackjack`) had implicit return types. Made all explicit with the full union (`{ data: BlackjackActionResult | null; error: string | null; active?: boolean } | { data: null; error: string; active?: boolean }`) so consumers get full type-checking. (Behaviour unchanged; same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH.)
  2. `mapHand` used unsafe `as number[]` casts for `playerCards`/`dealerCards` and `as BlackjackPlayerHandView[] | undefined ?? []` for `playerHands` — if the server returned a non-array (corrupted payload, schema drift, malformed edge-function response), these would propagate as-is and crash on `.map`/`.length` in the React render. Added `asNumberArray`/`asPlayerHands` helpers that `Array.isArray`-validate and normalize each field, with safe `Number()`/`Boolean()` coercion per field on `playerHands` rows. (Same defensive-parsing pattern KENO_MINES applied to `lib/mines.ts` `mapGameRow`.)
- **Roulette.tsx — 3 fixes** (race-safety + cleanup + error-path):
  1. `handleBet` double-bet race: No `spinning` guard at entry — relied solely on the Bet button's `disabled={spinning || !user}` prop, which left a sub-ms race window between the first click's `setSpinning(true)` state commit and the second click's handler execution. Fix: added `spinningRef = useRef(false)`; `handleBet` checks `if (spinningRef.current) return;` and sets `spinningRef.current = true` synchronously before any async work. Reset to `false` in both error and success paths. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH bugs #3, #9.)
  2. `await wait(remaining)` + subsequent `setState` calls had no unmount guard — if the user navigated away during the 1.6s spin delay, the state setters (`setDisplayPocket`/`setDisplayColor`/`setSpinning`/`setHistory`/`setLastResult`/`setPfNonce`) fired on a dead component. Fix: added `cancelledRef = useRef(false)` set to `true` in the unmount cleanup; `handleBet` checks `if (cancelledRef.current) return;` after each `await` before calling setState. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH bug #5.)
  3. `refreshProfile` not called in bet-error path — if `placeRouletteBet` returned an error, the balance display was stale (server may have debited before failing). Fix: added `void refreshProfile();` in the bet-error branch. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH bugs #6, #15.)
- **Roulette.css — 1 addition**:
  1. Added `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block disabling decorative CSS animations (`roulette-disc-spin` infinite spin, `roulette-ball-bounce`, `roulette-hub-dot` pulse, `roulette-spinner`, `roulette-result-in` pop) and the settling transition on `.roulette-wheel__disc-wrap--spinning .roulette-wheel__disc`, `.roulette-wheel__disc-wrap--settling .roulette-wheel__disc`, `.roulette-wheel__ball`, `.roulette-wheel__hub-dots span`, `.roulette__spinner`, `.roulette__result`. A static `transform: rotate(var(--spin-from, 0deg))` fallback is applied to the spinning disc so it still renders at a fixed angle (no infinite rotation). The hub number/color is still shown, the result text is still displayed, and the wheel disc still snaps to the result rotation (just without the 1.2s cubic-bezier transition). Verified the rule loads via `curl` of the served CSS chunk. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH `Limbo.css`/`Crash.css` reduced-motion blocks.)
- **Blackjack.tsx — 4 fixes** (race-safety + cleanup + error-path + 🔴 critical coinType forwarding):
  1. `handleStart` and `runAction` double-click race: No `busy` guard at entry — same sub-ms race window as Roulette/Limbo/Crash. Fix: added `busyRef = useRef(false)`; both `handleStart` and `runAction` check `if (busyRef.current) return;` and set `busyRef.current = true` synchronously before any async work. Reset to `false` in both error and success paths. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH bugs #3, #9.)
  2. `await startBlackjack(...)` / `await fn(...)` + subsequent `setState` calls had no unmount guard — if the user navigated away during the server round-trip, the state setters fired on a dead component. Fix: added `cancelledRef = useRef(false)` set to `true` in the unmount cleanup; both `handleStart` and `runAction` check `if (cancelledRef.current) return;` after each `await` before calling setState. The `resume` callback (called on mount if `user`) also checks `cancelledRef.current` after `fetchActiveBlackjack()` so a navigation during the resume fetch doesn't fire `applyHand` on a dead component. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH bug #5.)
  3. `refreshProfile` not called in `handleStart`/`runAction` error paths — balance display was stale if the server debited before failing. Fix: added `void refreshProfile();` in both error branches. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH bugs #6, #15.)
  4. 🔴 **Critical: insurance action silently dropped `coinType`**. The inline lambda for the insurance branch was `(id: string) => insuranceBlackjack(id, Boolean(insuranceTake))` — it only accepted `id` and ignored the `coinType` argument that `runAction` passes via `fn(hand.handId, coinType)`. This meant insurance always used the default `"balance"` coinType even when the user was playing in Sweeps Coins mode — the server would debit GC instead of SC, and the SC balance wouldn't reflect the insurance wager. TypeScript didn't catch this because the union of function types `(id: string) => ... | (id: string, ct?: string) => ...` is callable with `(string, string | undefined)` (extra args are silently ignored by the narrower signature). Fix: changed the lambda to `(id: string, ct?: string) => insuranceBlackjack(id, Boolean(insuranceTake), ct)` so the `coinType` is forwarded to `insuranceBlackjack(handId, take, coinType)` and onward to the `blackjack-game` Edge Function. (Verified the server-side `action === "insurance"` branch in `upload/lottacash_extracted/supabase/functions/blackjack-game/index.ts` reads `coinType` from the request body and uses it for the insurance-debit RPC, so the fix end-to-end is correct.)
- **Blackjack.css — 1 addition**:
  1. Added `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block disabling decorative CSS animations (`bj-card-deal` slide-in, `bj-result-in` slide-in, `.bj__card:hover` lift) on `.bj__card`, `.bj__result-banner`, `.bj__card:hover`. Cards still render at their final position (no entrance animation), the result banner still appears (no slide), and the totals are still shown — only the motion is suppressed. Verified the rule loads via `curl` of the served CSS chunk. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH `Limbo.css`/`Crash.css` reduced-motion blocks.)

- Verified the CaseBattles game-logic contract by reading `lib/games/case-battles/{index,config,engine,provablyFair,cases,caseTypes,bots,caseCatalog.generated}.ts` (consumed, not owned): gamemodes are `normal` (highest total unboxed wins), `group` (pot split equally among all human players), `terminal` (highest last-round drop wins), `jackpot` (one provably-fair roll, odds proportional to total unboxed). Player modes cover 1v1 / 1v1v1 / 1v1v1v1 / 1v1v1v1v1v1 / 2v2 / 2v2v2 / 3v3 / 2p / 3p / 4p / 6p. MAX_CASES_PER_BATTLE=50, MAX_COPIES_PER_CASE_TYPE=10, MAX_BORROW_PERCENT=80. `entryAfterBorrow` and `payoutKeepMultiplier` math is correct. The server-side winner determination and case-item drop rates live in the `case-battle` Edge Function (not in the repo's `upload/` dir, so I couldn't read it — but the client just displays server-returned `winningSlots`/`winnerSlot`/`winnerPayout`/`players[].drops`, so no client-side calc to audit).
- **lib/slots.ts — 1 fix** (type safety):
  1. `placeSlotsBet` had an implicit return type. Made it explicit `Promise<{ data: SlotsBetResult | null; error: string | null }>` so consumers get full type-checking. (Behaviour unchanged; same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH/ROULETTE_BJ for `lib/limbo.ts`/`lib/crash.ts`/`lib/roulette.ts`/`lib/blackjack.ts`.)
- **lib/caseBattles.ts — 3 fixes** (type safety + defensive parsing):
  1. All 8 exported functions (`caseBattleAction`, `listOpenCaseBattles`, `viewCaseBattle`, `claimCaseBattlePayout`, `createCaseBattle`, `joinCaseBattle`, `addBotToCaseBattle`, `fetchCaseBattlePfState`, `setCaseBattleClientSeed`) had implicit return types. Made all explicit with the full union so consumers get full type-checking. (Behaviour unchanged; same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH/ROULETTE_BJ.)
  2. `mapBattle` used unsafe `as CaseBattlePlayer[]` cast for `data.players` (well, `(data.players as CaseBattlePlayer[]) ?? []` — the `?? []` saves null/undefined, but a non-array truthy value like a string or object would propagate and crash consumers' `.map`/`.length`/`.some`/`.find`). Added `asCaseBattlePlayers(raw: unknown): CaseBattlePlayer[]` helper that `Array.isArray`-validates the outer array AND each player object AND each drop object, with safe `Number()`/`String()`/`Boolean()` coercion per field. (Same defensive-parsing pattern KENO_MINES applied to `lib/mines.ts` `mapGameRow` and ROULETTE_BJ applied to `lib/blackjack.ts` `mapHand`.)
  3. `mapBattle` used unsafe `as number[]` casts for `winningSlots` (both `data.winningSlots as number[]` and `results!.winningSlots as number[]`). Added `asNumberArray(raw: unknown): number[]` helper that `Array.isArray`-validates and `Number()`-coerces each entry, filtering non-finite values. (Same pattern as fix #2.)
- **Slots.tsx — 7 fixes** (race-safety + cleanup + error-path + correctness + a11y + reduced-motion + ref-sync):
  1. `handleSpin` double-spin race: No `rolling` guard at entry — relied solely on the Spin button's `disabled={rolling}` prop, which left a sub-ms race window between the first click's `setRolling(true)` state commit and the second click's handler execution. Fix: added `rollingRef = useRef(false)`; `handleSpin` checks `if (rollingRef.current) return;` and sets `rollingRef.current = true` synchronously before any async work. Reset to `false` in both error and success paths. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH bugs #3, #9 / ROULETTE_BJ bugs #4, #8.)
  2. `await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, remaining))` + `await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, lastReelDelay))` + subsequent `setState` calls had no unmount guard — if the user navigated away during the 1.2s reveal delay or the 0.6s reel-land delay, the state setters (`setLastResult`/`setShowResult`/`setRolling`/`setPfNonce`/`setReels`/`setReelStates`) fired on a dead component. Fix: added `cancelledRef = useRef(false)` set to `true` in the unmount cleanup; `handleSpin` checks `if (cancelledRef.current) return;` after each `await` before calling setState. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH bug #5 / ROULETTE_BJ bugs #5, #9.)
  3. `refreshProfile` not called in bet-error path — if `placeSlotsBet` returned an error, the balance display was stale (server may have debited before failing). Fix: added `void refreshProfile();` in the bet-error branch. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH bugs #6, #15 / ROULETTE_BJ bugs #6, #10.)
  4. 🔴 **Critical: `setPfNonce((prev) => (prev ?? 0) + 1)` was wrong.** The server returns `nonce` = the USED nonce for this bet (verified by reading `upload/lottacash_extracted/supabase/functions/place-slots-bet/index.ts` line 181: `nonce` in the response is the consumed nonce from `consume_keno_nonce`). The next expected nonce is `data.nonce + 1`. The old code did `(prev ?? 0) + 1` which accidentally worked on the very first bet (prev=null → 0 → 1, data.nonce=0 → next=1 ✓) but would DRIFT if the user had multiple sessions, if the server's nonce advanced past the displayed value (e.g. via another tab), or if `pfNonce` was stale for any reason. Fix: `setPfNonce(data.nonce + 1)` — authoritative from the server response.
  5. Spin button not disabled for `!user` — inconsistent with the KENO_MINES/LIMBO_CRASH/ROULETTE_BJ convention ("Bet/Deal button `disabled={!user}`"). The old code let guests click Spin, then `handleSpin` returned early with `setError("Log in to play.")`. Now the button is `disabled={rolling || !user}`, the label shows "Log in to play" when `!user`, and `aria-disabled` is set for screen readers. (Same convention as all other game pages.)
  6. Reduced-motion: the rAF spin animation (random symbol cycling at 55ms intervals) is purely decorative — the outcome is server-determined, the user has no timing decisions. For reduced-motion users, this rapid flickering is exactly the kind of motion that should be reduced. Fix: added `prefersReducedMotionRef = useRef(false)` populated from `window.matchMedia("(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)")` on mount; `handleSpin` checks the ref and skips `startRollAnimation()` + the staggered `stopRollAnimation()` landing, instead setting all reels to their final values simultaneously. The result is still shown after the REVEAL_DELAY_MS so the user perceives a brief "thinking" pause. (The decorative CSS animations — reel-shake, reel-land bounce, win-pulse — are suppressed via the Slots.css reduced-motion block in fix #7 below. This JS fix handles the rAF-driven random cycling that CSS can't reach.)
  7. `stopRollAnimation` did not directly update `reelStatesRef.current` — it only called `setReelStates(...)`, which triggers a re-render, which triggers the `useEffect(() => { reelStatesRef.current = reelStates; }, [reelStates])` sync AFTER paint. This left a one-frame gap where the rAF tick still saw `reelStatesRef.current[i] === "spinning"` for the just-landed reel and would overwrite its symbol with a random value via `setReels((prev) => prev.map((s, i) => reelStatesRef.current[i] === "spinning" ? Math.floor(Math.random() * 7) : s))`. Cosmetic (only ~1 frame of flicker per reel landing) but real. Fix: in the `setTimeout` callback, directly update `reelStatesRef.current = reelStatesRef.current.map((s, idx) => idx === i ? "landed" : s)` BEFORE the `setReels`/`setReelStates` calls, mirroring the existing pattern in `startRollAnimation` (which already does `reelStatesRef.current = ["spinning", "spinning", "spinning"]` directly).
- **Slots.css — 1 addition**:
  1. Added `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block disabling decorative CSS animations (`slots-reel-shake` infinite vibration, `slots-reel-land` bounce, `slots-win-pulse` infinite pulse) and transitions on `.slots__reel`, `.slots__reel--rolling .slots__reel-inner`, `.slots__reel--landed .slots__reel-inner`, `.slots__reel--win`, `.slots__reels--win .slots__win-line`. The reels still snap to their final landed position (no entrance animation), the win-line still highlights, and the outcome text is still announced via the `aria-live="polite"` region — only the motion is suppressed. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH `Limbo.css`/`Crash.css` and ROULETTE_BJ `Roulette.css`/`Blackjack.css` reduced-motion blocks.)
- **CaseBattlesHub.tsx — 2 fixes** (double-fetch + UX):
  1. 🔴 **Double-fetch on mount**: `useEffect(() => { setLoading(true); void loadLobby(); }, [loadLobby])` AND `useCaseBattlesLobbyPoll(true, loadLobby)` (which internally calls `tick()` → `loadLobby()` immediately on mount, then every `POLL_MS=2000ms`) BOTH fired on mount. So every navigation to the Hub made two parallel `listOpenCaseBattles(50)` Edge-Function calls within ~1ms. The first call's response was usually overwritten by the second. Fix: removed the explicit `useEffect` entirely — the poll hook is now the sole owner of the initial fetch + polling. The `loading` state is initialized to `true` in `useState(true)`, so the skeleton still shows on the first render before the poll hook's initial tick resolves. (Verified: skeleton still appears briefly on mount; no regression.)
  2. Error state shown alongside empty state: when `loadLobby` returned an error (e.g. "Supabase is not configured. Add your keys to .env."), the Hub rendered BOTH the error alert AND the "No open battles" empty state with a "Create battle" link. Confusing UX — the user sees a "create" CTA next to an error saying the backend is down. Fix: in the loading/error/empty/list conditional cascade, added an `error ? (<></>) :` branch between `loading` and `sortedBattles.length === 0` so the empty state is suppressed when there's an error. The error alert (rendered above the conditional) remains the sole UI in the error case. (Verified: Hub now shows ONLY the error alert, no duplicate empty state.)
- **CaseBattlesCreate.tsx — 3 fixes** (race-safety + cleanup + error-path):
  1. `handleCreate` double-click race: No `busy` guard at entry — relied solely on the Create button's `disabled={!canCreate}` prop (where `canCreate` includes `!busy`), leaving a sub-ms race window. Fix: added `busyRef = useRef(false)`; `handleCreate` checks `if (busyRef.current) return;` and sets `busyRef.current = true` synchronously before any async work. Reset to `false` in both error and success paths. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH/ROULETTE_BJ.)
  2. `await createCaseBattle(...)` + `await refreshProfile()` + subsequent `setBusy`/`navigate` had no unmount guard — if the user navigated away during the server round-trip, the state setters fired on a dead component. Fix: added `cancelledRef = useRef(false)` set to `true` in the unmount cleanup; `handleCreate` checks `if (cancelledRef.current) return;` after each `await` before calling setState/navigate. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH bug #5 / ROULETTE_BJ bugs #5, #9.)
  3. `refreshProfile` not called in `handleCreate` error path — balance display was stale if the server debited the entry before failing. Fix: added `void refreshProfile();` in the error branch. (Same pattern as LIMBO_CRASH bugs #6, #15 / ROULETTE_BJ bugs #6, #10.)
- **CaseBattlesRoom.tsx — 2 fixes** (polling cleanup + retry cleanup):
  1. Polling callback had no unmount/stale guard: the `useEffect`'s `setInterval` callback did `const { data } = await viewCaseBattle(battle.battleId); if (!data) return; setBattle(data); ...`. If the component unmounted (or the effect re-ran due to `battle?.battleId`/`battle?.status` changing) during the `await`, the cleanup only cleared the interval — the in-flight `viewCaseBattle` promise still resolved and called `setBattle(data)` on a dead component (or, worse, overwrote the NEW battle's state with the OLD battle's data when navigating between battles). Fix: added a local `let stale = false;` flag in the effect; the callback checks `if (stale) return;` after the `await` before any setState; the cleanup sets `stale = true` and clears the interval. (The `stale` flag is LOCAL to this effect — intentionally NOT shared with the initial-fetch effect's `cancelledRef` — so that an in-flight poll from the previous battleId doesn't accidentally bail out the new battleId's initial fetch.)
  2. The Retry button called `loadBattle(battleId)` WITHOUT the optional `cancelled` callback. `loadBattle` already accepted `cancelled?: () => boolean` (used by the initial-fetch effect via `() => stale`), but the Retry handler didn't pass it. So if the user clicked Retry then navigated away (or the battleId changed), the retry's `setBattle`/`setError`/`setLoading` fired on a dead component. Fix: added a component-level `cancelledRef = useRef(false)` shared between the initial-fetch effect and the Retry button; the initial-fetch effect resets it to `false` on mount and sets it to `true` on cleanup; the Retry button passes `() => cancelledRef.current` to `loadBattle`. (The polling effect keeps its OWN local `stale` flag for the reason noted in fix #1.)
- **CaseBattlesHub.css — 1 addition**:
  1. Added `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block disabling decorative CSS animations (`cbh-shimmer` skeleton pulse, `cbh-pulse` live-dot pulse) and transitions on `.cbh__card`, `.cbh__card:hover`, `.cbh__cases-stack .cb-page__case-chip`, `.cbh__card:hover .cb-page__case-chip`, `.cbh__fill-bar span`. Skeleton blocks still render as static faded bars; the live dot still shows (just doesn't pulse); the lobby list, badges, and metrics remain fully readable. (Same pattern as `Slots.css` reduced-motion block.)
- **CaseBattlesCreate.css — 1 addition**:
  1. Added `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block disabling decorative CSS animations (`cbc-rounds-window-in` slide, `cbc-fade-in` modal fade, `cbc-slide-up` modal slide) and transitions on `.cbc__case-slot`, `.cbc__case-slot:hover`, `.cbc-modal__case:hover`, `.cbc__mode-card`, `.cbc__player-btn`, `.cbc__summary-create`, `.cbc__summary-create:hover:not(:disabled)`. The case grid, toggles, summary card, and modal contents remain fully interactive. (Same pattern as `Slots.css` reduced-motion block.)
- **CaseBattlesRoom.css — 1 addition**:
  1. Added `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block disabling decorative CSS animations (`cbr-spin` loading spinner, `cbr-pulse` running-phase dot, `cbr-eos-spin` EOS-wait spinner, `cbr-rounds-window-in` slide, `cbr-drop-settle` last-drop bounce) and the phase-stripe transition on `.cbr__arena::before`. The reels' JS-driven transforms (case-open reel via `CaseOpenReel.tsx` and jackpot reel via `JackpotReel.tsx`) are NOT suppressed here — those animations are core gameplay (the user must watch the reel land on their item / the jackpot pointer land on the winner) and are preserved, consistent with the LIMBO_CRASH precedent of preserving the rAF multiplier curve. The reels do declare `will-change: transform` but that's a no-op when no animation runs.
- **Verification with agent-browser** (desktop 1280×800, then mobile 375×667, then reduced-motion):
  - `/#/slots`: renders cleanly; Spin button now correctly `disabled` and labeled "LOG IN TO PLAY" (`!user`); reels show "—" placeholders; wager input + ½/2×/MAX adj buttons; fairness panel toggles open. No new console errors. (Verified `button "LOG IN TO PLAY" [disabled, ref=e20]` in the accessibility tree.)
  - `/#/case-battles`: renders cleanly; hero with "0 OPEN" / "0.00 GC IN POTS" stats; "Active battles" panel with sort dropdown; error alert "Supabase is not configured. Add your keys to .env." shown WITHOUT the duplicate "No open battles" empty state (verified: only `alert` + `paragraph "Need funds? Deposit to join battles."` in the panel, no `cbh__empty` block).
  - `/#/case-battles/create`: correctly redirects to `/#/login?redirect=%2Fcase-battles%2Fcreate` (verified via `window.location.hash`); login page renders with the auth-config-warning note and disabled "Log in" button.
  - `/#/case-battles/some-fake-id`: gracefully shows the error alert + Retry button + "Back to battles" link. No new console errors.
  - Mobile 375×667 (`/#/slots` + `/#/case-battles`): no layout breakage, no console errors. Screenshots saved to `/tmp/slots-mobile.png` (59 KB) and `/tmp/cb-hub-mobile.png` (103 KB). Reset to 1280×800 after.
  - Reduced-motion (`agent-browser set media light reduced-motion` then `agent-browser set media light`): `/#/slots` renders with `LOG IN TO PLAY` button disabled, reels static. `/#/case-battles` renders with the error alert (no skeleton pulse, no live-dot pulse). Both pages' `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` CSS blocks are present in the served CSS chunks (Turbopack dev-mode lazy-loads CSS chunks per-route). Verified `window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches === true` during the reduced-motion pass.
  - Navigation `/#/slots` → `/#/case-battles` → `/#/case-battles/create` → `/#/case-battles/some-fake-id` → `/#/slots`: no errors, no leaks, no stuck states. Filtered console output for `slots|case-battle|cbr|cbh|cbc|error|exception|uncaught` (excluding pre-existing Supabase/GoTrueClient/THREE/HMR noise) → zero matches.
  - **Note on play-through verification**: Because Supabase is unconfigured in the sandbox (`useAuth().user === null`), the Spin/Create button is `disabled` on Slots and Create, and the Hub shows the error alert instead of a battle list. I could not exercise the actual bet → spin → reveal → payout flow or the create → join → battle → payout flow end-to-end via the browser. The critical fixes were verified by code-trace: (a) Slots #1 — `handleSpin` checks `rollingRef.current` at entry before any async work; (b) Slots #2 — `handleSpin` checks `cancelledRef.current` after each `await` before any setState; (c) Slots #4 — `setPfNonce(data.nonce + 1)` is authoritative from the server response (verified the server returns the USED nonce via the Edge Function source); (d) Slots #6 — `prefersReducedMotionRef` is read on mount and gates `startRollAnimation`/`stopRollAnimation`; (e) CaseBattlesRoom #1 — the polling callback checks `stale` after `await viewCaseBattle` before any setState; (f) CaseBattlesRoom #2 — the Retry button passes `() => cancelledRef.current` to `loadBattle`. I also confirmed the server-side contracts by reading `upload/lottacash_extracted/supabase/functions/place-slots-bet/index.ts`: the response includes `nonce` (used nonce), `reels`, `symbols`, `won`, `multiplier`, `payout`, `outBalance`, `gameId`, `coinType` — all matching the `SlotsBetResult` type.
- **TypeScript**: `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` reports zero errors in any owned file. (Only remaining TS errors in the repo are the pre-existing `lib/supabase.ts` SupabaseClient generic mismatch owned by FOUND-1, and `upload/lottacash_extracted/` / `examples/` / `skills/` Deno server files — all outside SLOTS_CASEBATTLES scope.)
- **Cross-file impact verification**: `lib/slots.ts` is consumed only by `pages/Slots/Slots.tsx` (my scope). `lib/caseBattles.ts` is consumed by `pages/CaseBattles/{CaseBattlesHub,CaseBattlesCreate,CaseBattlesRoom,CaseBattleArena,CaseBattleJoinPanel}.tsx` — the Arena and JoinPanel are supporting files (NOT in my owned set), but my lib return-type tightenings are purely additive (explicit annotation on previously-inferred types) and the defensive `asCaseBattlePlayers`/`asNumberArray` parsing produces the SAME `CaseBattleView` shape (just safer), so all consumers' destructuring `const { data, error } = await viewCaseBattle(...)` / `data.players.some(...)` / `data.winningSlots.includes(...)` is unchanged. `lib/games/case-battles/*` and `lib/games/case-battles/config.ts` are consumed by my pages + by the CaseBattleArena — but my edits to these shared files were zero (audited clean, no changes), so no cross-game impact.

Stage Summary:
- Files audited: pages/Slots/Slots.tsx, pages/Slots/Slots.css, pages/CaseBattles/CaseBattlesHub.tsx, pages/CaseBattles/CaseBattlesHub.css, pages/CaseBattles/CaseBattlesCreate.tsx, pages/CaseBattles/CaseBattlesCreate.css, pages/CaseBattles/CaseBattlesRoom.tsx, pages/CaseBattles/CaseBattlesRoom.css, pages/CaseBattles/CaseBattlesPages.css, pages/CaseBattles/CaseOpenReel.css, pages/CaseBattles/JackpotReel.css, lib/slots.ts, lib/caseBattles.ts (13 owned files); plus 18 consumed (non-owned) supporting files in `pages/CaseBattles/` audited for contract correctness with zero edits, and 8 consumed game-logic files in `lib/games/case-battles/` audited clean with zero edits.
- Bugs found: 22
  1. lib/slots.ts: `placeSlotsBet` implicit return type → consumers couldn't easily name the result type.
  2. lib/caseBattles.ts: all 8 exported functions had implicit return types → same as above.
  3. lib/caseBattles.ts: `mapBattle` used unsafe `as CaseBattlePlayer[]` cast for `data.players` → would crash on `.map`/`.length`/`.some`/`.find` if server returned a non-array (corrupted payload / schema drift).
  4. lib/caseBattles.ts: `mapBattle` used unsafe `as number[]` casts for `winningSlots` (both `data.winningSlots` and `results.winningSlots`) → same crash risk.
  5. Slots.tsx: `handleSpin` lacked `rolling` ref guard at entry → double-spin race window between click and re-render.
  6. Slots.tsx: `await wait(remaining)` + `await wait(lastReelDelay)` + subsequent setState had no unmount guard → setState on dead component if user navigated away during the 1.2s reveal delay or the 0.6s reel-land delay.
  7. Slots.tsx: `refreshProfile` skipped on bet-error path → balance display stale if server debited before failing.
  8. Slots.tsx: 🔴 **critical** — `setPfNonce((prev) => (prev ?? 0) + 1)` was wrong; the server returns the USED nonce, so the next nonce is `data.nonce + 1`. The old code drifted if the user had multiple sessions or if the server's nonce advanced past the displayed value.
  9. Slots.tsx: Spin button not disabled for `!user` — inconsistent with the KENO_MINES/LIMBO_CRASH/ROULETTE_BJ convention; guests could click Spin and get an error message instead of a disabled button.
  10. Slots.tsx: rAF spin animation (random symbol cycling at 55ms) not skipped for reduced-motion users — purely decorative motion that should be suppressed.
  11. Slots.tsx: `stopRollAnimation` didn't sync `reelStatesRef.current` directly (only via setState + useEffect-after-paint) → one-frame race where the rAF tick overwrote the just-landed reel's symbol with a random value. Cosmetic but real.
  12. Slots.css: no `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block → decorative CSS animations (reel-shake, reel-land bounce, win-pulse) played even when the user had reduced motion enabled.
  13. CaseBattlesHub.tsx: 🔴 **critical** — double-fetch on mount; `useEffect(() => { void loadLobby(); }, [loadLobby])` AND `useCaseBattlesLobbyPoll(true, loadLobby)` (which fires `tick()` immediately on mount) both called `loadLobby` on mount. Two parallel `listOpenCaseBattles(50)` Edge-Function calls within ~1ms on every navigation to the Hub.
  14. CaseBattlesHub.tsx: error alert + empty state both rendered simultaneously when `loadLobby` returned an error — confusing UX (user sees a "Create battle" CTA next to an error saying the backend is down).
  15. CaseBattlesCreate.tsx: `handleCreate` lacked `busy` ref guard at entry → double-click race.
  16. CaseBattlesCreate.tsx: `await createCaseBattle` + `await refreshProfile` + setState/navigate had no unmount guard → setState on dead component during server round-trip.
  17. CaseBattlesCreate.tsx: `refreshProfile` skipped on `handleCreate` error path → balance display stale if server debited before failing.
  18. CaseBattlesRoom.tsx: polling callback had no `stale` flag → `setBattle(data)` fired on a dead component if the user unmounted/navigated during the 600-1500ms `await viewCaseBattle`, or overwrote the NEW battle's state with the OLD battle's data when navigating between battles.
  19. CaseBattlesRoom.tsx: Retry button called `loadBattle(battleId)` without the `cancelled` callback → `setBattle`/`setError`/`setLoading` fired on a dead component if the user clicked Retry then navigated away.
  20. CaseBattlesHub.css: no `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block → decorative shimmer + live-dot pulse played for reduced-motion users.
  21. CaseBattlesCreate.css: no `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block → decorative rounds-window slide + modal fade/slide played for reduced-motion users.
  22. CaseBattlesRoom.css: no `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block → decorative spinners (loading, EOS wait), pulses, drop-settle bounce played for reduced-motion users.
- Bugs fixed: 22 (all of the above). lib/games/case-battles/* and the 18 supporting files in pages/CaseBattles/ had zero bugs in scope (audited as consumed dependencies, not in owned set).
- Cross-cutting requests:
  - **CaseOpenReel.tsx + JackpotReel.tsx reduced-motion (supporting files, NOT in owned set)**: The case-open reel and jackpot reel animations are JS-driven via inline `track.style.transition = "transform ${durationMs}ms ${easing}"` and last 2-5 seconds (configurable via `ROUND_DELAY_MS=5000` / `ROUND_DELAY_FAST_MS=2000`). For reduced-motion users, the reel still plays at full duration — snapping via a CSS `!important` override would leave the user staring at a static reel for the full 5 seconds before the `setTimeout(done, durationMs + 80)` fires and the next round starts (worse UX than watching the reel). Properly handling this requires editing `CaseOpenReel.tsx` and `JackpotReel.tsx` to detect `prefers-reduced-motion` via `window.matchMedia` and shorten the duration (e.g., to 200ms) and the corresponding `setTimeout` delay. These files are NOT in my owned set — flag for coordinator.
  - **CaseBattleArena.tsx deep audit (supporting file, NOT in owned set)**: The Arena (783 LOC) is the actual live-battle UI (player columns, case-open reels, jackpot reel, EOS-wait overlay, final results, payout claim, add-bot, playback bootstrap/sync). I audited it briefly for contract correctness with my `lib/caseBattles.ts` changes (the `asCaseBattlePlayers` defensive parsing is compatible with all Arena consumers) but did NOT deep-audit its internal state machine (playback bootstrap, sync decisions, payout-claim race, add-bot pending set, pagehide reload marking, etc.). If the coordinator wants a dedicated CaseBattleArena audit, that's a separate task — the file is too large to fold into this audit's owned set.
  - **CaseBattles realtime (Supabase channels)**: The task description mentioned "CaseBattles multiplayer won't work without Supabase realtime" and "Supabase realtime subscriptions cleaned up on unmount". The current implementation uses HTTP polling (every 600ms-2000ms via `setInterval`) — NO `supabase.channel(...)` / `onPostgresChanges` / `.subscribe()` calls anywhere in `pages/CaseBattles/` or `lib/caseBattles.ts`. So there are no realtime subscriptions to clean up; the polling cleanup is correctly handled (intervals cleared on unmount, `stale` flag for in-flight requests — see fixes #18, #19). If the coordinator wants TRUE realtime, that would require adding `supabase.channel('case_battles').on('postgres_changes', { event: '*', schema: 'public', table: 'case_battles', filter: `id=eq.${battleId}` }, ...).subscribe()` in `CaseBattlesRoom.tsx` + a server-side realtime publication on the `case_battles` table (`alter publication supabase_realtime add table case_battles;`) + handling the `joined`/`left`/`started`/`completed` payload variants. A coordinator-level feature, not a bug fix.
  - **Guest/fun mode (same as KENO_MINES + LIMBO_CRASH + ROULETTE_BJ cross-cutting request)**: Games are browse-only for guests (`disabled={!user}` on the Spin/Create button + global `GameGuestBanner`). Not fixable at the page-audit level — requires a `FunModeContext` with a fake balance + local-only game-round functions using the client-side provably-fair logic (the slots reel selection algorithm is already in the Edge Function and could be mirrored client-side; the case-battles drop algorithm is server-side only and would need to be ported). Recommend the coordinator dispatch a dedicated FUN_MODE task spanning all 7 game pages + a new context + the Topbar.
  - **Stale success/error banners never auto-clear (same as KENO_MINES + LIMBO_CRASH + ROULETTE_BJ + ACCOUNT + WALLET cross-cutting request)**: Slots and CaseBattlesCreate set `error` state that persists until the next user action. A shared "auto-dismiss after N seconds" hook (or moving to Toast-based feedback via `useToast().promise()`) would be a coordinated refactor across all game pages.
- Remaining issues: none within owned files. The pre-existing `lib/supabase.ts` SupabaseClient generic TS error is owned by FOUND-1 and out of SLOTS_CASEBATTLES scope. The CaseOpenReel/JackpotReel reduced-motion gap and the CaseBattleArena deep-audit gap are coordinator-level decisions (see cross-cutting requests).

   1. Replaced `useCallback(load, [tab]) + useEffect(load, [load])` pattern with an inline `useEffect` carrying a local `cancelled` flag. The previous version had no unmount guard — if the user navigated away during the `await fetchBiggestWins(50)` / `fetchMostWagered(50)` round-trip (or rapidly switched tabs), the in-flight promise would still resolve and call `setEntries`/`setLoading` on a dead (or now-wrong-tab) component. The new cleanup sets `cancelled = true` and the async callback checks `if (cancelled) return;` before any setState. (Same pattern as SLOTS_CASEBATTLES / LIMBO_CRASH / ROULETTE_BJ / KENO_MINES fixes.)
   2. Added current-user row highlight: imports `useProfile`, compares each entry's `username` to `profile.username` (case-insensitive), applies `leaderboard-table__row--me` class + a `you` badge pill. Skips gracefully when `profile.username` is null (guests see no highlight — verified at runtime). New CSS in Leaderboard.css: `.leaderboard-table__row--me td` gets a crimson tint + inset left stripe (overrides the top-3 gold/silver/bronze tint); `.leaderboard-table__you` is a small uppercase crimson pill with `aria-label="(you)"` for SR users.

The Trophy icons inside are `aria-hidden` by default (verified via lucide-react source), so SR users previously heard nothing for ranks 1–3 (the rank number is visually replaced by the medal icon and was invisible to AT). Now SR users hear "Rank 1" / "Rank 2" / "Rank 3".

The `--me` row tint overrides the top-N tint via CSS source-order specificity (both selectors have the same specificity, but `--me` is declared after `--top1/2/3` in the file). Mobile (`@media max-width:640px`) block already hides `__secondary` (Win Rate) column — no additional mobile rules needed for the new classes.

7. **pages/Help/Help.tsx — 3 fixes** (ARIA accordion pattern + tablist keyboard nav + key collision):

Added proper ARIA accordion pattern to FAQ items: each question `<button>` now has `id={buttonId}`, `aria-controls={panelId}`; each answer `<div>` now has `id={panelId}`, `role="region"`, `aria-labelledby={buttonId}`. Previously the button had only `aria-expanded` and the answer had no `role`/`id`/`aria-labelledby` — AT users couldn't navigate from the button to its associated panel. Now verified at runtime: clicking the "What are Gold Coins (GC) and Sweeps Coins (SC)?" button produces a `region "What are Gold Coins (GC) and Sweeps Coins (SC)?"` element in the accessibility tree, properly labelled.
   2. Added proper tablist pattern: tabs now have `id`, `aria-controls` (pointing to the panel id), and `tabIndex={tab === x ? 0 : -1}` (only the active tab is in the tab order; the WAI-ARIA Authoring Practices Guide for tabs specifies roving tabindex). The panels now have `id` and `aria-labelledby` (pointing back to the tab id). Previously the panels had only `role="tabpanel"` and `aria-label` (textual label, not associated to the tab) — now they're properly wired bidirectionally.
   3. Fixed React `key` collision on TOS meta paragraphs: was `key={trimmed.slice(0, 24)}` (collided if two meta paragraphs shared the same first 24 chars). Changed to `key={`meta-${i}`}` (uses the array index, which is stable for this static content split). Same fix applied to Privacy.tsx and SweepstakesRules.tsx (below).

   2. Fixed `key` collision (same as Help.tsx fix #3): `key={trimmed.slice(0, 24)}` → `key={`meta-${i}`}`.

Work Log:
- Read worklog.md (all 1057 lines, including FOUND-1..9 stage summaries) and dev.log tail to establish context. Key takeaways: (a) HashRouter with `/#/route` URLs; (b) FOUND-1 hardened `isSupabaseConfigured` so placeholders resolve to `false`; (c) FOUND-1 set the convention of guarding every Supabase-calling lib function with `isSupabaseConfigured` and returning a clean `{ error: "<msg>" }` so callers never see a confusing network/CORS error against the placeholder host (see `lib/notifications.ts createUserNotification`); (d) Settings.tsx (FOUND-9?) documented a subtle React Router bug: when a component returns `<Navigate>`, it stays mounted for one more render and `useLocation().pathname` returns the NEW pathname on that second render, clobbering the original `?redirect=` param — the fix is to hardcode the redirect path. Confirmed the same bug existed in AdminRoute (verified live with agent-browser: navigating to `/#/admin` redirected to `/#/login?redirect=%2Flogin` instead of `?redirect=%2Fadmin`).
- Established baseline with agent-browser on `/#/admin` (desktop 1280×800): redirected to `/#/login?redirect=%2Flogin` — the redirect-clobber bug. Login page rendered correctly with disabled submit button (FOUND-1's fix). No new console errors from admin-owned files.
- Audited `components/AdminRoute/AdminRoute.tsx`: (1) Redirect-clobber bug — fixed by replacing `loginUrl(pathname)` with hardcoded `loginUrl("/admin")` (mirrors the Settings.tsx fix). (2) Flash-of-wrong-redirect — when `user` becomes truthy (login completes) there's a render where `authLoading=false`, `profileLoading=false` (initial ProfileContext state), `profile=null` → AdminRoute fell through to `!profile?.isAdmin` and redirected to `/`. Fixed by adding `waitingForProfile = Boolean(user) && !profile` to the loading condition. (3) Wrapped `children` in `<>` for consistency with ProtectedRoute. (4) Removed unused `useLocation` import. (5) Added a JSDoc comment block noting client-side checks are convenience only; real authz is server-side RLS+RPC.
- Audited `components/ProtectedRoute/ProtectedRoute.tsx`: dead code (not imported by App.tsx — pages use inline `<Navigate to={loginUrl("/path")}>` instead). The same redirect-clobber bug existed (`useLocation().pathname` read on second render after Navigate). Since ProtectedRoute is generic, the redirect path can't be hardcoded — fixed with a `useRef` that captures the pathname on first render and is only updated when pathname is not `/login`. Also imported `loginUrl` from `authRedirect` for open-redirect sanitization (was raw `encodeURIComponent`). Added JSDoc explaining the ref pattern and the dead-code status. Added `role="status" aria-live="polite"` to the loading state.
- Audited `lib/admin.ts`: (1) **No `isSupabaseConfigured` guards** — every function called `supabase.rpc(...)` unconditionally, which (with FOUND-1's placeholder detection) would make a network request to `https://placeholder.supabase.co` and fail with a confusing CORS/network error. Fixed by adding `isSupabaseConfigured` guards at the top of all 9 functions, returning a clean `{ data: null, error: { message: "Admin tools are unavailable — Supabase is not configured…", code: "NOT_CONFIGURED" } }` (mirrors FOUND-5's `createUserNotification` pattern). (2) **No input validation** — functions like `completeAdminWithdrawal("")`, `adminCreditUser("", -5, ...)`, `processAdminRedemption("", "approve")` would all hit Supabase with bad params. Added explicit validation (non-empty IDs, positive amounts, coin-type / status / action enum checks) returning `{ error: { message, code: "INVALID_INPUT" } }`. (3) **Inconsistent return shape** — `fetchAdminStats` etc. returned `{ data, error }` from a destructured call, but `completeAdminWithdrawal` etc. returned `supabase.rpc(...)` directly (different inferred type). Unified all 9 functions under a single typed contract: `AdminResult<T> = { data: T | null; error: AdminError | null }` where `AdminError = { message: string; code?: string }`. Callers in Admin.tsx destructure `{ error: err }` and read `err.message` — works with both `PostgrestError` and the new `AdminError`. (4) Replaced `as any`-style casts on row fields with explicit null-coalescing. (5) Defined const tuples `VALID_WITHDRAWAL_STATUSES` / `VALID_REDEMPTION_STATUSES` / `VALID_COIN_TYPES` and derived union types from them so the runtime check and the type signature stay in sync.
- Audited `pages/Admin/Admin.tsx`:
  1. **Self-admin check missing** — `toggleAdmin` had a comment "You cannot change your own admin status here." but no enforcement. A user could revoke their own admin (locking themselves out) or — if the server didn't enforce — try to escalate themselves. Added `currentUser` from `useAuth()` and an early-return guard `if (currentUser && u.id === currentUser.id) { setUserSearchError("You cannot change your own admin status."); return; }` in `toggleAdmin`. The corresponding row also renders a "(you)" tag and the revoke/make-admin button is `disabled` with a tooltip. (Defense-in-depth: server RLS must also enforce, but this prevents accidental self-lockout.)
  2. **No confirmation for redemption reject** — clicking "Reject" on a redemption silently called `processAdminRedemption(r.id, "reject")`. Rejecting a redemption is a destructive action (user doesn't get paid; SC refunded). Added a `window.confirm()` dialog for both approve and reject, with action-specific messaging. Refactored the two inline arrow handlers into a single `handleRedemptionAction(r, action)` async function.
  3. **No confirmation for credit user** — `handleCredit` directly called `adminCreditUser` after only a numeric validation. Manual balance credits are irreversible from the admin UI (only a compensating debit would reverse it). Added `window.confirm(`Credit ${amount} ${coinLabel} to user ${uid}?`)` before the RPC call.
  4. **Premature "empty" message during loading** — the deposits table showed "No credited deposits yet." while `loading=true` (initial empty `deposits` state); the redemptions list showed "No pending redemptions." while `redemptionsLoading=true`. Fixed: deposits section now renders a `lc-loading` spinner while `loading`; redemptions section renders a spinner while `redemptionsLoading` (new state — `loadRedemptions` now sets it true/false around the fetch). Empty messages only render when loading is false AND the list is empty.
  5. **`loadRedemptions` silently swallowed errors** — `if (!err && data) setRedemptions(data)` dropped the error. Added `redemptionsError` state, surfaced the error in a `role="alert"` banner, and set loading false in a `finally`-style block.
  6. **`loadDashboard` didn't set loading=false when starting** — only set it false at the end. Added `setLoading(true)` at the top so the Refresh button's `disabled={loading}` actually disables during re-fetches (previously the Refresh button was clickable again immediately, allowing overlapping fetches).
  7. **`loadDashboard` didn't surface deposits error** — `if (!depositsRes.error) setDeposits(...)` silently dropped the error. Now sets `setError(depositsRes.error.message)` so a deposits-only failure is visible.
  8. **`actionError` not cleared before redemption actions** — `handleRedemptionAction` now calls `setActionError(null)` before the RPC.
  9. **After redemption action, only `loadRedemptions()` was called** — stats (pending count) and dashboard weren't refreshed. Changed to `Promise.all([loadRedemptions(), loadDashboard()])` so the stats panel stays in sync.
  10. **Stale `txHashes` entries** — after completing a withdrawal, the txHash for that withdrawal ID was left in state. Now removed via a functional `setTxHashes((prev) => { delete next[w.id]; return next; })` cleanup after successful completion.
  11. **Fragile error-vs-success classification for credit status** — used `creditStatus.includes("Error") || creditStatus.includes("error")` to decide whether to render the banner with `admin__banner--error`. Replaced with an explicit `creditIsError` boolean state. The banner's `role` is now `alert` on error / `status` on success, with matching `aria-live` (assertive/polite).
  12. **`copyText` swallowed clipboard rejections silently** — `void navigator.clipboard.writeText(text)` would silently reject in non-secure contexts (HTTP) or when the Clipboard API is denied. Wrapped in `try/catch` with a comment noting the destination address is still visible in the `<code>` element for manual selection-copy.
  13. **`handleFail` confirm message used raw `$${w.usdAmount}`** — showed e.g. "$1234.5" instead of properly formatted "$1,234.50". Switched to `formatUsd(w.usdAmount)`.
  14. **A11y: unlabeled inputs** — the user-search `<input type="search">`, the credit-user UUID input, the credit amount, the coin-type `<select>`, and the credit note input all had only `placeholder` attributes (no `<label>` or `aria-label`). Added `aria-label` to each. Added `role="search"` to the user-search `<form>`.
  15. **A11y: deposits table** — added `aria-label="Recent deposits"` and `scope="col"` to all `<th>` cells for screen-reader column-header association.
  16. **A11y: section headings** — added `aria-labelledby` on each `<section>` pointing to its `<h2 id="admin-...-title">` so screen readers announce the section context. (Page already had a correct h1 → h2 hierarchy.)
  17. **A11y: withdrawal card context** — added `aria-label` on each `<article>` withdrawal card so AT users hear "Withdrawal for <user>" when navigating by article.
  18. **A11y: copy button** — added `aria-label="Copy destination address"` (was just the text "Copy" with no context).
  19. **TS: `creditCoinType` was a bare `string`** — narrowed to `"balance" | "sweeps_coins"` union so the `<select>` onChange can't drift out of the valid set.
  20. **HTML entity fix** — `Fail & refund` was rendered as `Fail &amp; refund` in JSX (literal `&`), now correctly `Fail &amp; refund`.

Leaving the file in place since it's in my owned set and the task said "audit and fix" — deleting it would be a structural decision for the coordinator.

This means on the render immediately after `user` becomes truthy, `profileLoading` is still `false` and `profile` is still `null`. AdminRoute now defensively treats `user && !profile` as still-loading, which papers over the gap. A cleaner fix would be for ProfileContext to initialize `profileLoading = !!user` or to synchronously set `profileLoading = true` at the top of the bootstrap effect — but ProfileContext is owned by FOUND-1. The client-side `AdminRoute` + `is_current_user_admin` check is a convenience; the RPCs MUST each enforce `is_current_user_admin()` server-side and the underlying tables MUST have RLS policies that deny non-admin reads/writes. Verifying this is a server-side audit task, not a client-side one. Flagging for the coordinator.

Current state: search ✓ (users), refresh ✓ (withdrawals), filter-by-status ✓ (withdrawals/redemptions hardcoded to "pending"). Missing: pagination (deposits table is capped at 15 rows via `p_limit: 15` server-side; withdrawals/redemptions return all pending — could grow unbounded if many accumulate), CSV export. Adding pagination requires server-side cursor/offset params on the RPCs (out of scope — `supabase/` is not in my owned set). Adding CSV export is a client-only change but a feature add, not a bug fix; left it out to keep changes surgical. Flagging in case the coordinator wants to commission an ADMIN-FEATURES task.

The ProfileContext initial-`profileLoading` gap is owned by FOUND-1 (papered over client-side in AdminRoute). Server-side RLS/RPC authorization verification is a server-side audit task. Work Log:

Key takeaways reused: (a) HashRouter with `/#/route` URLs; (b) FOUND-1 fixed `isSupabaseConfigured` so placeholders resolve to `false`; (c) the global `GameGuestBanner` (rendered by `AppShell`) is the canonical guest UX — no game page renders its own auth overlay (GameAuthOverlay/GameFeedback are unused reserved components); (d) `invokeEdgeFunction` already short-circuits with `"Supabase is not configured. Add your keys to .env."` when not configured; (e) FOUND-2 flagged the `enrichChatMessagesWithLevels([msg]).then(([enriched]) => …)` destructure in SidebarChat.tsx as fragile-if-empty-array — addressed in chat.ts; (f) ACCOUNT agent flagged the Discord OAuth HashRouter redirect-URI incompatibility — already resolved in `lib/discord.ts` (see verification below).
- Read all 9 owned files end-to-end (4 components + 5 lib files). Cross-referenced consumers: AppShell.tsx (mounts GameGuestBanner + AffiliateRefCapture + calls useSessionReminder), SidebarChat.tsx (consumes chat.ts), Settings.tsx (consumes discord.ts + responsibleGaming.ts). Also read contexts/{Auth,Toast}Context.tsx, lib/{supabase,affiliateRef,authRedirect,leveling}.ts, types/chat.ts to verify the contracts my files depend on. Compared each owned file against its original Vite-era counterpart in `upload/lottacash_extracted/` via `diff` to understand what the migration + prior work had already changed.
- Established a baseline with agent-browser: home (`/`), game page (`/#/keno`), affiliate capture (`/?ref=ABC123` → localStorage `lottacash_affiliate_ref = "ABC123"`), malformed affiliate (`/?ref=abc-def!ghi` → `"ABCDEFGHI"`), Discord OAuth callback (`/?code=X&state=Y` → re-routed to `/#/settings?code=X&state=Y` → Settings.tsx redirects to `/#/login?redirect=%2Fsettings` since not authenticated in sandbox), sidebar chat mode (shows "Chat is unavailable — Supabase is not configured." notice). All degrade gracefully with zero page errors.
- **GameAuthOverlay.tsx — 1 fix** (focus thrash on parent re-render):
  1. The modal lifecycle `useEffect` had deps `[isModal, onClose]`. When a parent passed an inline (non-`useCallback`-memoized) `onClose`, every parent re-render would: (a) run cleanup → restore focus to `previouslyFocused.current` (the trigger button); (b) re-run effect → stash `document.activeElement` (now the trigger) and move focus back into the dialog. The user would see a visible focus flicker (trigger → dialog) on every parent state change. Fixed by introducing `onCloseRef` (kept in sync via a tiny `useEffect`), changing the lifecycle effect's deps to `[isModal]` only, and updating the Esc handler + backdrop-click handler to call `onCloseRef.current?.()`. The effect now runs once per modal mount/unmount regardless of `onClose` identity changes, while still calling the latest callback.
- **GameFeedback.tsx — 3 fixes**:
  1. The JSDoc comment claimed `aria-labelledby points at the visible label`, but the parent `<motion.div>` had no `aria-labelledby` attribute — only the `<p>` child had `id={labelId}`. Screen readers had no programmatic association between the live region and its label. Added `aria-labelledby={labelId}` to the outer `motion.div`.
  2. The auto-dismiss timer `useEffect` had deps `[durationMs, onDismiss]`. When a parent passed an inline `onDismiss`, every parent re-render cleared and re-created the `setTimeout`, restarting the countdown. If the parent re-rendered frequently during the dismiss window (e.g., updating `amount`/`multiplier` as part of the same win sequence), the timer would effectively never elapse. Fixed by introducing `onDismissRef` (kept in sync via a tiny `useEffect`), changing the timer effect's deps to `[durationMs]` only, and having the timeout callback invoke `onDismissRef.current?.()`.
  3. The internal `<AnimatePresence>` wrapping a single always-rendered `<motion.div>` does NOT enable exit animations — framer-motion only runs `exit` variants for direct children of `<AnimatePresence>` that are removed from inside it, and this component is unmounted as a whole by the parent's conditional (the inner AnimatePresence can't intercept that). The previous "safety net" comment was misleading. Rewrote the JSDoc to accurately document that parents MUST wrap their conditional `<GameFeedback />` in their own `<AnimatePresence>` for exit animations, and that the internal one is retained only for enter-animation safety.
- **GameGuestBanner.tsx — 1 fix** (redundant effects + lint):
  1. Two overlapping `useEffect`s both read `sessionStorage` and called `setDismissed` synchronously, triggering React 19's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule twice. Consolidated into a lazy `useState(readDismissedFromStorage)` initializer (eliminates the mount-only effect and its lint error) + a single `useEffect([loading, user])` that re-reads sessionStorage when auth state changes (handles the sign-out → guest transition and cross-tab dismissal). The remaining lint error on the auth-state effect is a false positive — sessionStorage is an external source that fires no same-tab events, so polling it in an effect when deps change is the standard pattern (documented in a comment). Extracted `readDismissedFromStorage()` helper to avoid duplicating the try/catch. Behavior unchanged: banner shows on game pages for guests, dismiss button writes `sessionStorage["lottacash_guest_banner_dismissed"] = "1"` and hides the banner for the rest of the session, reappears on next session.
- **AffiliateRefCapture.tsx — no code changes**. Audited: the two-effect structure is correct and non-redundant. Effect 1 (mount-only) reads `window.location.search` to capture `?ref=` from the ROOT URL (HashRouter's `useSearchParams` only sees the hash portion, so `/?ref=CODE` is invisible to it) and also calls `handleDiscordCallbackRedirect()` to re-route OAuth responses. Effect 2 (`[searchParams]`) captures `?ref=` from hash search params for in-app navigations like `/#/signup?ref=CODE`. Both call `normalizeAffiliateCode` (uppercase A–Z/0–9, max 32 chars — handles malformed values) and `storeAffiliateRef` (in `lib/affiliateRef.ts`, WALLET-owned). SSR-safe via `typeof window === "undefined"` guards. Runs once per mount (Effect 1 `[]` deps) / once per searchParams change (Effect 2). Cross-cutting request: `storeAffiliateRef` in WALLET-owned `lib/affiliateRef.ts` always overwrites and has no expiry — see cross-cutting requests below.
- **lib/useSessionReminder.ts — 1 fix** (silent session-clock reset):
  1. `const toast = useToast()` captured the WHOLE ToastContext value object, which is recreated whenever the toast LIST changes (every toast added/dismissed anywhere in the app). The `useEffect` dep `[user, toast]` therefore re-ran on every toast change: cleanup called `resetSessionTracking(user.id)` (deleting the session-start timestamp), then re-run called `trackSessionActivity(user.id)` (setting a NEW start). The session-duration clock was silently reset to zero every time ANY toast appeared or was dismissed during the session — so the 1-hour responsible-gaming reminder would never fire as long as any toast appeared during that hour (and toasts appear constantly: "Supabase not configured" warning, bet confirmations, etc.). Fixed by destructuring `const { info } = useToast()` — `info` is a `useCallback`-memoized function that is stable across toast-list changes — and depending on `[user, info]` instead. The interval now persists for the full session.
- **lib/responsibleGaming.ts — no changes**. Audited: all six Supabase-touching functions (`fetchSelfExclusion`, `createSelfExclusion`, `cancelSelfExclusion`, `fetchDepositLimits`, `setDepositLimits`) guard with `isSupabaseConfigured` and return clean `{ error: NOT_CONFIGURED_ERROR }` / `null` when unconfigured. `mapSelfExclusion` / `mapDepositLimits` defensively coerce unknown RPC rows with `Number.isFinite` checks. `setDepositLimits` validates NaN early. Per-user session tracking (`sessionStartByUser` Map) correctly keys by `userId` so switching users doesn't inherit the prior user's session start. No `any` types. No bugs.
- **lib/edgeFunctions.ts — no changes**. Audited: `invokeEdgeFunction<T>` is properly generic with `InvokeResult<T>` return type. Timeout via `timeout: timeoutMs` passed to `supabase.functions.invoke`. Retry logic (`retryOnTransient`) only retries `FunctionsFetchError` (transient network/timeout/DNS) — never `FunctionsHttpError` or `FunctionsRelayError` (which may have already processed the request) — with a clear JSDoc warning that it's only safe for idempotent operations. Error parsing handles all three supabase-js error classes + a legacy duck-typed fallback, with specific messages for 404 (deploy hint), 503 (BOOT_ERROR / redeploy hint), and timeout (aborted cause detection). `parseInvokeBody` handles `{ error }`, `{ code, message }`, and `BOOT_ERROR` shapes. No `any` types (uses `unknown` + narrowing). No bugs.
- **lib/chat.ts — 1 fix** (null-row crash hardening):
  1. `rowToChatMessage(row: Record<string, unknown>)` would throw `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null` if a Realtime `postgres_changes` payload or a supabase-js insert/select returned `null`/`undefined` for a row (callers cast via `as Record<string, unknown>` which TypeScript trusts but doesn't enforce at runtime). The SidebarChat consumer guards `if (!payload.new) return;` for the Realtime path, but the `fetchRecentChatMessages` path (`data.map((row) => rowToChatMessage(row as Record<string, unknown>))`) and `sendChatMessage` path (`rowToChatMessage(data as Record<string, unknown>)`) don't guard. Hardened the function signature to accept `Record<string, unknown> | null | undefined` and return a sentinel message (empty `id`, "Player" username, empty body) for nullish input. The sentinel is naturally filtered by SidebarChat's `prev.some((m) => m.id === enriched.id)` de-dup check (no real message has an empty id). This also indirectly addresses FOUND-2's cross-cutting note about the `enrichChatMessagesWithLevels([msg]).then(([enriched]) => …)` destructure — the function always returns a same-length array for non-empty input (via `.map()`), so the destructure is safe in practice; the nullish-row hardening is belt-and-suspenders.
- **lib/discord.ts — no changes**. Audited: the **Discord OAuth HashRouter issue flagged by ACCOUNT agent is already resolved**. `getDiscordRedirectUri()` returns `${window.location.origin}/` (the root URL, not `/settings`) — Discord OAuth2 forbids fragments in redirect_uri (RFC 6749 §3.1.2), so `/#/settings` can't be used directly. `handleDiscordCallbackRedirect()` (called by `AffiliateRefCapture` on first paint) detects `?code=&state=` at the root path and `window.location.replace`s to `${origin}/#/settings?code=&state=`, preserving the OAuth response so Settings.tsx's `useSearchParams` picks it up. Verified end-to-end: `agent-browser open "http://localhost:3000/?code=TESTCODE123&state=TESTSTATE456"` → URL becomes `http://localhost:3000/#/login?redirect=%2Fsettings` (the OAuth response was re-routed to `/#/settings?code=…&state=…`, then Settings.tsx redirected to login because Supabase is unconfigured in the sandbox → no session). CSRF protection via `generateStateToken()` (crypto.randomUUID with getRandomValues + Math.random fallbacks for insecure contexts), state stored in `sessionStorage` and validated one-time by `validateDiscordState()` (fail-closed if storage unavailable). `startDiscordOAuth()` throws if `sessionStorage.setItem` fails (avoids proceeding without CSRF protection). `unlinkDiscordAccount()` guards with `isSupabaseConfigured`. `isDiscordConfigured` is a simple `Boolean(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_DISCORD_CLIENT_ID)` export consumed by Settings.tsx to disable the Link button + show a hint. No `any` types. No bugs.
- **TypeScript**: `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` reports zero errors in any owned file (the only remaining TS error in `src/lottacash/` is FOUND-1's `lib/supabase.ts` SupabaseClient generic mismatch, outside GAME_UI scope).
- **ESLint**: 1 remaining `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint error on GameGuestBanner's auth-state effect — false positive (sessionStorage is an external source that fires no same-tab events; polling it in an effect when `loading`/`user` change is the standard React pattern). Documented in a comment. The lazy `useState` initializer eliminated the other lint error.

Stage Summary:

  1. GameAuthOverlay.tsx: focus thrash on parent re-render — `[isModal, onClose]` deps caused focus to flicker (trigger → dialog) on every parent state change when `onClose` was inline.
  2. GameFeedback.tsx: `aria-labelledby` claimed in JSDoc but never applied to the live-region element — screen readers had no programmatic label association.
  3. GameFeedback.tsx: auto-dismiss timer restarted on every parent re-render when `onDismiss` was inline — timer could effectively never elapse.
  4. GameFeedback.tsx: internal `<AnimatePresence>` JSDoc claimed it was a "safety net" for exit animations — misleading; it cannot intercept the parent's unmount of the whole component.
  5. GameGuestBanner.tsx: two overlapping `useEffect`s both called `setDismissed` synchronously (2 `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint errors) — consolidated into lazy `useState` initializer + one auth-state effect.
  6. useSessionReminder.ts: `useToast()` returned the whole context value object (recreated on every toast-list change) — `[user, toast]` deps caused the session-duration clock to silently reset to zero every time any toast appeared/dismissed, so the 1-hour reminder would never fire.
  7. chat.ts: `rowToChatMessage` would crash on `null`/`undefined` input (callers cast via `as` which TypeScript doesn't enforce at runtime) — hardened to return a sentinel message for nullish rows.
- Bugs fixed: 7 (all of the above).
- Discord OAuth HashRouter issue resolved: yes (already resolved by prior work; verified end-to-end with agent-browser — `/?code=&state=` correctly re-routes to `/#/settings?code=&state=` via `handleDiscordCallbackRedirect` in `AffiliateRefCapture`).
- Cross-cutting requests:
  1. **`lib/affiliateRef.ts` (WALLET-owned) — affiliate ref overwrite + no expiry**: The GAME_UI task spec says AffiliateRefCapture should "store in localStorage with expiry, doesn't overwrite existing." The WALLET agent audited this file as "clean" and made no changes, but `storeAffiliateRef()` always overwrites (via `localStorage.setItem`) and has no expiry. If a user clicks affiliate link A, then later clicks affiliate link B (without signing up), the ref changes from A to B — the first ref is lost. Recommend WALLET agent add: (a) a `getStoredAffiliateRef()` check in `storeAffiliateRef` to skip overwriting an existing non-expired ref, and (b) a companion timestamp key (e.g., `lottacash_affiliate_ref_ts`) with a 30-day TTL check in `getStoredAffiliateRef()`. My AffiliateRefCapture component calls `storeAffiliateRef` as-is; the fix belongs in the storage layer.
  2. **`pages/Settings/Settings.tsx` (ACCOUNT-owned) — Discord OAuth response lost on expired session**: If a user's session expires between starting Discord OAuth and the callback, `handleDiscordCallbackRedirect` correctly re-routes to `/#/settings?code=&state=`, but Settings.tsx's effect (`if (!code || !session) return;`) returns early without consuming the code, then `<Navigate to={loginUrl("/settings")}>` fires and the `?code=&state=` are permanently lost from the URL (the redirect URL only carries `?redirect=%2Fsettings`, not the OAuth params). After re-login the user lands on `/settings` with no OAuth response. Recommend ACCOUNT agent preserve the OAuth params across the login redirect (e.g., stuff them into sessionStorage before the Navigate, or include them in the `redirect` query param). Edge case but unrecoverable when it happens.
  3. **`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule (coordinator/config-owned)**: The new React 19 lint rule flags the legitimate "sync external storage when deps change" pattern in GameGuestBanner as an error. This is a false positive — sessionStorage fires no same-tab events, so polling it in an effect is the standard React approach (the alternative, `useSyncExternalStore`, can't handle same-tab writes). Recommend the coordinator confirm this is acceptable or add a scoped eslint-disable comment convention. The current code has a comment explaining why the pattern is correct.
- Remaining issues: none within owned files. The `enrichChatMessagesWithLevels([msg]).then(([enriched]) => …)` destructure in SidebarChat.tsx (FOUND-2's cross-cutting note) is safe in practice — `enrichChatMessagesWithLevels` always returns a same-length array for non-empty input via `.map()`, so `[enriched]` is never `undefined`. The chat.ts nullish-row hardening is belt-and-suspenders. GameAuthOverlay and GameFeedback are currently unused (reserved components) — verified via ripgrep that no page imports them; the fixes are forward-looking for when they're wired into a game page.

- Sticky footer verified on short page: **yes** — `/#/redeem` at 1280×800: `footer.bottom = 800 = viewportH`, `gap = 0px`, `footer.margin-top = 95.19px` (auto margin pushes footer to bottom of viewport).
- Sticky footer verified on long page: **yes** — `/#/sweepstakes` at 1280×800: `footer.bottom = 2336` (below viewport), `main.scrollHeight = 2272 > main.clientHeight = 736`, `footer.margin-top = 0px` (content overflows, auto margin = 0); after scrolling main to bottom: `footer.top = 341, footer.bottom = 800 = viewportH, gap = 0px` (footer reachable at end of scroll, no floating gap).
- No horizontal scroll on mobile: **yes** — tested 8 routes (`/`, `/sweepstakes`, `/privacy`, `/help`, `/leaderboard`, `/originals`, `/keno`, `/mines`) at 375×667; `html.scrollWidth === innerWidth === 375` on every route.
- Cross-cutting requests:
  1. **Footer.css breakpoint inconsistency (FOUND-2-owned)**: `src/lottacash/components/Footer/Footer.css` uses `@media (max-width: 768px)` for its mobile layout, while `layout.css` and `lc-pages.css` use `@media (max-width: 900px)`. Between 768–900px the rest of the app is in mobile-drawer mode (sidebar off-canvas, topbar hamburger visible, page padding reduced) but the footer is still in desktop 3-column layout. Recommend FOUND-2 change Footer.css's `@media (max-width: 768px)` to `@media (max-width: 900px)` (and the `@media (max-width: 480px)` to `@media (max-width: 480px)` — that one is already consistent) so the footer transitions to mobile at the same breakpoint as the rest of the chrome. The desktop footer's `flex-wrap: wrap` makes the visual impact minor, but the inconsistency is real.
  2. **Render-blocking Google Fonts `@import` (coordinator / `src/app/layout.tsx`-owned)**: The `@import url('https://fonts.googleapis.com/...&display=swap')` at the top of `theme.css` already includes `&display=swap` (so the font FILES are non-blocking) but the `@import` directive itself is render-blocking — the browser must fetch the Google Fonts CSS (~1KB gzipped) before rendering any text. The modern pattern is to remove the `@import` from `theme.css` and instead add to `src/app/layout.tsx`'s `<head>`: `<link rel="preconnect" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com">`, `<link rel="preconnect" href="https://fonts.gstatic.com" crossorigin>`, and `<link rel="stylesheet" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Syne:wght@400;500;600;700;800&family=Rajdhani:wght@400;500;600;700&family=JetBrains+Mono:wght@400;500&display=swap">`. `src/app/layout.tsx` isn't in any agent's owned set (it was touched during the initial migration), so the coordinator should decide who owns it. Minor impact — left as-is.
  3. **GameAuthOverlay / GameFeedback z-index trapping (FOUND-4-owned)**: `GameAuthOverlay.css` and `GameFeedback.css` declare `position: fixed; z-index: var(--lc-z-overlay, 1000)` (now resolving to 1000 via the new theme.css token), but both components are rendered inside `<main class="app-shell__main">` which has `position: relative; z-index: 1` (layout.css line 38-47) — establishing a stacking context. Because main IS a stacking context, the overlay's z-index:1000 is trapped below the topbar (z-index:100 globally). If/when FOUND-3/GAME_UI wire these overlays into a live game page, they'll appear BEHIND the topbar. The clean fix is for FOUND-4 to portal the overlays to `document.body` via `createPortal` (component-side change), not a CSS change. Currently the overlays are unused reserved components (per FOUND-3 + GAME_UI stage summaries), so this is latent — flagging for FOUND-4 to address when they activate the components.
- Remaining issues: none within owned files. The `.lc-field` / `.lc-field__label` / `.lc-field select` rules in `global.css` and the `.lc-btn--primary` / `.lc-btn--secondary` / `.lc-btn--outline` aliases in `lc-pages.css` are technically dead (0 `.tsx` consumers) but are intentionally retained as a documented public design-system API for future pages — removing them would create inconsistency (since `.lc-btn--ghost` IS used) and the byte cost is negligible. The Google Fonts `@import` render-blocking behavior is documented as cross-cutting request #2.
